# Odyssey Launcher: architecture

A fully 3D game launcher frontend, in the same space as Playnite, ES-DE and RetroBat, styled after the PS2 memory card browser:

- The main screen is a 3D grid of systems. Selecting one opens a 3D grid of that system's games, and selecting a game launches it in the configured emulator.
- Every item is a floating 3D model, and the focused item animates.
- Each system and theme defines an icon.sys-style background (a four-corner gradient, with an optional wallpaper over it) and lighting (three directional lights plus ambient).
- Default game models are per-system box templates textured with scraped art. Users can replace any model with their own `.glb`.

Windows comes first, Linux later if it's cheap, and macOS never. **Performance is the headline feature** (see A3).

Milestones and acceptance criteria are in [ROADMAP.md](ROADMAP.md). Decisions are recorded at the end of this file.

## A1. Layout and modules

```
OdysseyLauncher/
  OdysseyLauncher.slnx       Debug / ExportDebug / ExportRelease (Godot's configurations); Godot 4.6+ finds .slnx files
  global.json                .NET SDK 10.0.x; dotnet test uses Microsoft.Testing.Platform
  Directory.Build.props      Version, Nullable=enable, ExportRelease => Optimize=true (Godot's SDK only does this for its own project)
  src/Launcher.Core/         net10.0 class library, no Godot reference (a test enforces this)
  tests/Launcher.Core.Tests/ xUnit v3
  godot/                     project.godot, OdysseyLauncher.csproj (-> Launcher.Core), src/, scenes/, shaders/, sounds/ (the navigation sounds), themes/ (the app's own themes: memory-card, the base, and console; ignored by Godot, copied beside the exe on export by addons/odyssey_export)
  tests/themes/              user themes for tests and captures (M6: slot-showcase)
  tests/themes-list/         a made-up theme list (themes.json and screenshots) for the downloader's captures and tests
  samples/themes/            user themes for theme authors to copy (M6: retro-tv; docs/THEMING.md)
  src/Launcher.Updater/      OdysseyUpdater.exe, the update helper (2026-10-18): Native AOT, shipped beside the launcher
  tools/verify.ps1           build, test, Godot headless build/import/smoke run
  tools/linux/               the Linux AppImage (2026-11-02): the tools in WSL or on the release's runner, FFmpeg's build (L4), the tests on Linux, the packaging (AppStream metadata, zsync: L5), its checks
  .github/workflows/release.yml  the Windows and Linux releases (2026-11-05: the zip, the AppImage), on a pushed v<version> tag
  docs/  CLAUDE.md
```

### Launcher.Core (namespace `Launcher.Core.*`)

| Namespace | Responsibility |
|---|---|
| `Config` | `ConfigLoader` loads TOML (Tomlyn), layering user files over built-in defaults (embedded resources). <br>Maps Tomlyn's syntax tree by hand into a tree that keeps every key's position, so diagnostics still point at the right file after merging (M2). <br>Merges recursively; scalars and arrays are replaced. <br>Validates, with file:line:column:key diagnostics; unknown keys get a "did you mean" warning. <br>Parsers take text plus a source name, so the app can feed `res://` files from the PCK. <br>The files the app ships (the built-in defaults, and the built-in theme) are read by `TomlFast`, a strict parser for the plain TOML they use (tables, arrays of tables, bare keys, strings, numbers, booleans and single-line arrays): Tomlyn took about 50 ms for the built-in catalogue (A5), and `TomlFast` about 1 ms. It builds the same tree with the same positions (`TomlFastTests` compares them), and anything it doesn't handle, or TOML forbids, returns null so Tomlyn parses the text and reports the problem. The user's files always go through Tomlyn. <br>The install checks (an emulator's program or core missing) aren't part of boot: `ConfigSources.FileExists` is null there, and the app loads config again after `interactive` with `CheckInstallsFor`, the systems that have games (`AppServices.CheckInstallsAsync`, and again after each rescan), replacing the settings screen's list of problems. <br>From M7, `ConfigWriter` writes the settings screen's changes through `TomlEditor`, an editor over Tomlyn's syntax tree, so comments, blank lines, key order and quoting survive; only values that differ from the built-in defaults are written, and the edited files must load with no new error before anything is written (A5 Writing config). `ConfigInput` checks what the user entered first (the folder exists, the program is an .exe, a credential is one line). |
| `Data` | `LibraryDatabase` and `UserDatabase` (create, migrate, back up), `MigrationRunner`, and the connection helpers. <br>**Microsoft.Data.Sqlite's async API is synchronous**, so reads run on the thread pool, and writes go through one dedicated writer thread (`DbWriter`). <br>Readers come from our own pool (`ReaderPool`), not Microsoft.Data.Sqlite's: each has `userdata.db` ATTACHed once and is `query_only`, and the pool can be closed and held back while a rebuilt library file is swapped in. <br>Every connection sets WAL, `foreign_keys=ON`, `synchronous=NORMAL` and `busy_timeout`. |
| `Scanning` | `RomScanner` walks a system's ROM folders by file name (extensions, recursion, excludes). Several folders per system are allowed; the first folder wins a `path_key` collision. <br>Folders are listed with a 256 KB buffer, and `LibraryService` scans 8 systems at once on dedicated threads, because a network share costs a round trip per listing refill. Excluded folders (by default ES-DE's `images`, `manuals` and `videos`) aren't listed at all. <br>From 2026-10-10, **folder games**: a folder whose name ends in one of the system's extensions (PS3's `Game.ps3`, PS4's `Game.ps4`, PS5's `Game.ps5`) is a game and isn't entered, as ES-DE's "directories interpreted as files" (`ScannedFile.IsFolder`, size 0: its contents are never listed). The library doesn't record it; the stages that care ask the disk when they run (`FolderGame`): the launch gives the emulator the folder's file of its own name if it holds one (`Jet Grind Radio.cue/Jet Grind Radio.cue`), else the folder (RPCS3 and shadPS4 take one), ScreenScraper's hash is skipped, the details screen's size reads "A folder", and the game can't be deleted from the launcher (A4 Deleting a game). <br>Multi-file games: files referenced by an `.m3u`, `.cue` or `.gdi` are hidden. Parsed playlists are cached in `library.db` by size and mtime, so a rescan doesn't re-read them. <br>`TitleParser` turns No-Intro and Redump names into a display title and a sort key, keeping region, languages, revision, disc and other tags as separate fields. <br>Normalises paths to `rel_path` and `path_key`. <br>`MediaScanner` (M5's `UserMedia`; from 2026-10-02 the whole media folder) indexes `DataDir/media/<system>/<folder>/<name>.<ext>` (A4) into `media`: images (PNG, JPEG or WebP), videos (`video`, MP4), manuals (`manual`, PDF; 2026-10-06) and per-game models (`model`, `.glb`), scraped or the user's own alike. A file matches the game with the same `path_key`, or every game whose `path_key` is its name plus an extension. From 2026-10-06 the scan is given the system's games (the ROM scan's, or the library's for a media-only refresh): a file no game would match is skipped before anything is read, and a system with no games isn't listed at all. Each file's size and time are stored (migration 0002), so a rescan reads only new or changed image headers; a video's, a manual's or a model's header isn't read. <br>From 2026-10-03 each ROM's creation time is kept too (`games.added_ms`, from the folder listing the scan already reads; no extra call per file on Windows), for sorting games by when they were added. |
| `Scraping` | (M4) `IScraper` for ScreenScraper, IGDB and SteamGridDB, the Steam store (2026-10-01, no credentials) and LaunchBox's Games DB (2026-11-01, no credentials: looked up in a local copy of its database, `LaunchBoxDatabase`; A5 LaunchBox Games DB), each with a capability map (the fields and media kinds it can supply). <br>`ScrapeService`: scrape a game, a system, or everything missing, and clear a game, as operations with progress events (raised on worker threads). They go through a persistent queue in `userdata.db` (resumed after the app closes; single games jump ahead; cancellable), and games run concurrently within each provider's limits. <br>`ProviderGate` bounds each provider's requests in flight, per second and per minute, and holds them during a rate-limit pause or after a used-up quota; `ScraperHttp` retries rate limits and transient failures with exponential backoff, honouring Retry-After. <br>Provider selection: `[scraping] provider` first, then each `fallback` in order, asked only for what's still missing and what its capability map has; a provider with no credentials is skipped and reported. <br>`ProviderAccounts` reads credentials from `secrets.toml` and `ODYSSEY_*` variables. <br>Match resolution (A2). With `--save-responses` (2026-10-02; off by default), each provider's response is saved in `scraped/responses/` (credentials redacted), to debug a scrape, and `ScrapedRestore` rebuilds a game's scraped metadata and matches from them offline when a scan adds it. <br>From M7: `TestConnectionAsync`, one cheap request per provider with the current credentials (ScreenScraper `ssuserInfos.php`, or `ssinfraInfos.php` without an account; IGDB its cached token and the smallest query; SteamGridDB and the Steam store a search; LaunchBox a HEAD request for its dump), which never throws for a refusal; and `CountMissingAsync`, what "scrape all missing" would take on, per system. From M7 part 2, `CountSystemAsync` (what "scrape this system" would take on: its games, and how many are missing). From 2026-10-04, `ScrapeSystemAsync` takes a `SystemScrapeFilter`: every game, or only those without a front cover, without a screenshot, or not scraped within `ScrapeService.RecentScrape` (30 days, whatever the outcome; never scraped counts), and `CountSystemAsync` counts each in the same query (`ScrapeStore.CountSystem`). <br>**Manual matching** (2026-10-01): `SearchMatchesAsync` searches every provider at once (`IScraper.SearchAsync`) for the game's title (the user's, else the file name's) or a typed name, and returns each provider's results, closest name first (`TitleMatcher.Similarity`), with its current match, or why it couldn't be searched; `ScrapeGameWithMatchAsync` saves the result the user chose as a manual match and scrapes the game with it first (batch kind `manual`; see A2's match resolution). From 2026-10-07 each result carries its front cover (`MatchCandidate.Cover`), which `GetMatchCoverAsync` downloads small, only when asked (A2, Manual matching). |
| `Media` | (M4) `MediaStore`: scraped downloads, written to the media folder as the game's file (`media/<system>/<folder>/<name>.<ext>`, from 2026-10-02; from 2026-10-06 in ES-DE's folders and named without the ROM's extension, A4) atomically (a temporary file, then a move), never replacing a file the game has of that kind (A4), with the image checked by its first bytes (a video, from 2026-10-01: an MP4, by its `ftyp` box; a manual, from 2026-10-06: a PDF, by `%PDF-` in its first KB; neither gets a derivative). <br>`Bc7Encoder` (modes 6 and 1) and `Bc7DdsWriter` make the derivative; `DerivativeBaker` decodes (`IImageDecoder`), squeezes to 512² and encodes; `DerivativeService` bakes on its own below-normal thread, and `BakeMissingAsync` bakes every image without a derivative (every kind from M6, since any can fill a theme's slot) and deletes stale ones. From 2026-10-04, `ThemeLogos`: a theme's system logos (A6 System cards' logos), described from their headers, baked by `DerivativeService.BakeFileAsync` on logo threads of its own (half the processors, at most 4, below normal, made on first use; the library's thread helps, taking logos before library images) into `CacheDir/theme-logos/textures/`, apart from the library's derivatives, named by the theme's id and the path in it (`LogoFile.Key`, so an editor run and an export share them), and pruned there. <br>From 2026-10-04, `MediaFolder`: where the media folder is (`[paths] media`, else `DataDir/media`; `LibraryService.MediaDir` follows the current config) and a stored path's file (its `media/` stands for the folder wherever it is); and `MediaFolderMove`, which moves its files to another folder (A4 The media folder's location). <br>From M5: `ImageHeaders` reads a PNG, JPEG or WebP's size from its header only; `TextureDerivatives` names each baked derivative (`CacheDir/textures/<key>.dds`, the key a SHA-256 of the source's path, size and time; from 2026-10-02 every source is in DataDir, so no root), allocation-light so texture workers can call it per cover; `MediaKinds` lists the kinds and each one's folder (`FolderOf`). <br>From 2026-10-02, `IVideoDecoder`: a game's video decoded for its details screen, as RGBA frames (`IVideoReader`, with its size, pixel shape and length, and a seek) and 32-bit float sound (`IAudioReader`), each reader used on the one thread that opened it (Windows: `MediaFoundationVideoDecoder`; Linux, from L4: `FfmpegVideoDecoder`; both in `Platform`). <br>From 2026-10-27, `IPdfRenderer`: a game's manual rendered page by page for its viewer and thumbnails, as RGBA (`IPdfReader`, with its page count and each page's size), each reader used on the one thread that opened it (Windows: `WindowsPdfRenderer`; Linux, from L4: `PdfiumRenderer`; both in `Platform`). <br>From M7 part 2, `UserArtService`: the user's own image for one of a game's slots, chosen in its options. From 2026-10-27 also its video (an MP4) and its manual (a PDF), checked by their first bytes as scraping checks them, with no derivative; removing either works as removing an image does. It's copied to the game's name in the media folder, `DataDir/media/<system>/<folder>/<name>.<ext>` (from 2026-10-06 the ROM's name without its extension, so every ROM of that name shares it, as ES-DE names media; one file per kind, so the game's file in another format or named after its whole ROM name goes, a scraped one too), its derivative is baked, and the game's files of that kind are indexed again without a ROM scan or a listing of the media folder (`RefreshGameMediaAsync`, which raises `MediaChanged`; decisions log, 2026-11-11). Removing deletes the game's files of that kind (named with or without the ROM's extension), whoever put them there, so every ROM of that name loses it, and the slot stays empty until a scrape fills it. |
| `Importing` | (2026-10-04) A system's ES-DE `gamelist.xml`, imported (A4 Imported gamelists). `GamelistReader` reads one (a fragment, as ES-DE writes `<alternativeEmulator>` beside `<gameList>`; DTDs refused; never throws), normalising each `<game>` as the user's edits are (`MetadataInput`: ES-DE's `19891101T000000` to `1989-11-01`, a 00 month or day dropping that part; a rating of 0, ES-DE's "not rated", left out; a value too long left out). `GamelistPaths` resolves its paths (`./`, bare, `~/`, absolute) against the ROM folder the gamelist is in, or each of the system's ROM folders for one kept elsewhere (ES-DE's `~/ES-DE/gamelists/<id>/`), to a game's `rel_path`. `GamelistImportService`: `FindAsync` (the system's ROM folders, then ES-DE's gamelists folder by id and alias), `PlanAsync` (read and matched against the library in one query: what it would add, for the question asked first) and `ImportAsync` (the titles, metadata, favourites and matches in batches of 200 on the writer, then each file copied, an image's derivative queued, the media folder indexed every 100 files after their bakes). |
| `Theming` | (M6) `ThemeLoader` parses and validates a theme manifest (A6) from text plus a source name, over `IThemeFiles` (a folder on disk), with file:line:column:key diagnostics like config's; a bad block falls back to the base theme's, a bad template is left out. From 2026-10-03 every theme is loaded over the **base theme**, `memory-card`: what it leaves out is the base's (its look blocks, `look_transition_ms`, a `game_template` naming a base template, a same-id template extended key by key; A6). `ThemeCatalog` finds the app's own themes (`<exe dir>/themes/<id>/`) and the user's (`ConfigDir/themes/<id>/`) and picks the active one: `slab` by default (2026-10-08; `console` before), never the base (it isn't offered, and its id is reserved), which stays the last resort. `ModelResolver` lists each system's model candidates for its games and its card in precedence order (A7), with its look and colour. `SlotChain` and `MediaSlots`: the media slots, their standard texture sizes, and fallback-chain resolution (allocation-free, for the grid). From 2026-10-15, `ThemeMenu` (a theme's `[ui.menu]`, key by key over the base's) and `MenuPalette`, every colour the menus draw with (`MenuRole`): `Legacy`, the launcher's own, exactly, unless the theme sets a colour, else `Derive`d from its background, text and accent (A6 Menus); from 2026-10-18 `OverlayPalette`, the same for the text over the grids. <br>From 2026-10-16, `Theming/Downloads`, the theme downloader (A6 Downloading themes): `ThemeIndexReader` (the list's `themes.json`, never throwing; a bad entry left out with a warning), `ThemeInventory` (what the user has of each listed theme, from the clone alone: no network), `ThemeDownloads` (the list refreshed; a theme installed, updated or removed, one operation at a time, on the thread pool), `ThemeCovers` (what a theme brings, counted for the list) and `AppVersion` (release versions, ordered as SemVer), over `IGitClient`, whose `LibGit2Client` is the one class that touches LibGit2Sharp. |
| `Models` | No Godot dependency. `GlbInfo` reads a `.glb`'s material names from its JSON chunk only (theme validation checks a template's slots before the app loads it). From M6 part 2: <br>`GlbFile` splits a GLB into its JSON (a mutable tree) and binary chunk, and writes one back. <br>`ModelInspector` validates a model against A7 without Godot: every index, accessor, buffer view and index value, the node tree (no cycles or shared children), embedded PNG or JPEG images sized from their headers, and required extensions; it counts triangles over the scene's nodes, materials, textures, joints and morph targets against `ModelBudget` for its `ModelKind` (game template, per-game, system model), and finds the slots and the clips (`ModelClips`). A file Godot could misread or crash on is rejected here. <br>`ModelProcessor` scales textures over the budget's side down (decoded and scaled by `IImageDecoder`, written back by `Media/PngEncoder`) and repacks the binary chunk. <br>`ModelCache` keeps processed user models and their reports in `CacheDir/models/`, keyed by path, size, time, kind and processing version, so a model (or a rejection) is processed once. <br>`ModelLog` is the log the user reads, `DataDir/logs/models.log`. <br>`ObjConverter` turns a Wavefront OBJ model (a zip, or an `.obj` with its `.mtl` and textures beside it) into a GLB through `GltfBuilder`, which also writes the synthetic library's and the tests' models. A PS2 save icon's shape animation beside the OBJ (`ICON.ICO.anim`, ps2iodb's JSON: whole-mesh frames blended by weight keys) is read by `ShapeAnimation` and written as morph targets and a `focused` clip of their weights. A PS2 save icon (an `.anim` or an `iconsys.json` beside the OBJ) faces −Z as ps2iodb exports it, so the converter turns it a half turn about Y to face +Z (2026-10-03). <br>`ModelImportService`, for M7's game options panel and `odyssey-scrape import-model`: import (convert, fit to the per-game budget, write to the game's model slot, prime the cache, index, log), remove, and the current model's report. From M7 part 2 it does the same for a system's own models (A7 level 2: its card, `models/systems/<system>.glb`, to the system model budget, and its game template, `models/templates/<system>.glb`, to the template budget), which aren't indexed: they're found when a theme is resolved. |
| `Launching` | `LaunchPlanner` is pure and static: it picks the emulator (this launch's choice, then the game's override, then the system's) and expands its templates into a final argument list (A5). A named emulator that isn't configured is an error, never a silent fallback. <br>**Title ids and installed games** (2026-10-28; A5 Vita3K's games): for a profile that uses `{title_id}` or whose emulator installs its games (`installs`), `LaunchService` reads the game's title id first (`TitleId`: the `[PCSF00024]` in its name, else a small file's text, else a `param.sfo`), and with `installs = "vita3k"` asks `Vita3KGames` whether Vita3K has it installed: one that is is planned with the profile's `args`, one that isn't with its `install_args`, or fails before anything starts when Vita3K can't install its file. The planner stays pure: it's given what was read (`LaunchPlanner.Resolve`, then `Plan` with the title id and the choice). <br>A profile with `run_file = true` (A5) runs the game's own file: the plan's `Executable` is the ROM, with no arguments, and `LaunchPlan.RunFile` is set, so `LaunchService` doesn't look for a program. <br>**Steam games** (2026-10-01): a `run_file` game whose file is a `.url` naming a Steam app (`SteamShortcut`: `steam://rungameid/<id>`, `run` or `launch`), or on Linux a `.desktop` file whose `Exec` line hands Steam one (L3b, 2026-11-04), is handed to Steam (the plan is `Detached`: the shell opens it and nothing it starts is followed, so a Steam client the shortcut starts isn't mistaken for the game) and followed by `SteamGame` through `ISteamClient`: running while Steam's current game is that app, or while the app's own running flag is set (trusted only once seen clear, as Steam can leave it set), and the client runs; ended after two polls without it (1 s apart); never started if it isn't seen running within 2 minutes, not counting time Steam spends updating it (`ProcessOutcome.NotStarted`, a failure that isn't a play). Cancelling stops following and leaves the game to Steam. Without Steam installed, the launch fails before anything starts. <br>`LaunchService` checks the ROM, executable, core and working folder exist, runs the plan through `IProcessRunner`, and records the play session through `IPlayHistory`. One game at a time. <br>Events, raised on worker threads: `Starting`, `Running` (process id), then exactly one of `Exited` (exit code, duration) or `Failed` (a reason written for the user). A non-zero exit within 5 s is `Failed`, and isn't counted as a play. Cancelling ends the game's whole process tree. (M3) <br>**Controllers** (2026-10-10): for a system with `auto_configure_controllers` whose emulator's profile has `controllers = "eden"`, `LaunchService` sets the connected pads up in Eden's `qt-config.ini` just before `Starting` (`Launching/Controllers`; A5 Eden's controllers). The app passes the pads (`Gamepads`: Godot's device, name and SDL GUID for each, the one holding A when the game was chosen first). `SdlGuid` reads a GUID as SDL does; `SdlMapping` works out the raw inputs SDL 3.4 binds for it (its XInput mapping, or the one it generates for a HIDAPI pad); `EdenControllers` gives each pad a player, works out Eden's names for it (the GUID without its CRC, and its port among pads of that GUID), and writes the bindings Eden's own SDL driver would choose. `Starting` carries what was done (`ControllerSetup`, logged as `Launch controllers for ...`), or why it couldn't be done; the game launches either way. |
| `Library` | `LibraryService`, the façade the app uses: systems summary (boot), games list (on entry), the Favourites, Recently played and All games lists, favourites and overrides, and rescan and rebuild jobs with progress, and metadata overrides (M4; scrape jobs are `ScrapeService`'s). A rescan lists every ROM folder, and indexes the media folders its `RescanMedia` says (2026-10-18): `All`, every scanned system's ("Rescan the library and media", Shift+F5, a media folder change, saved ROM folders), or `SystemsWithNewGames`, only those of systems where it found a game the library didn't have ("Rescan the library", F5, and the scans at launch; a first scan's games are all new), so an unchanged system's media folder isn't listed. Scans and rebuilds run one at a time; each writes in one transaction. <br>From M6: `GetGameMediaAsync` (a system's or chosen games' media of the kinds a theme's chains name, one indexed query), `RefreshMediaAsync` (one system's media folder indexed again without a ROM scan, for the gamelist import; M6's `RefreshUserMediaAsync`), `RefreshGameMediaAsync` (from 2026-11-11, for the art and model services: one game's files of one kind, and every game sharing their name, indexed again by looking up the names they can have, `MediaScanner.Probe`, not by listing the folder), and the `MediaChanged` event, raised after a rescan or refresh that changed media files, a scrape that saved media, a clear, a rebuild, or a bake. <br>From M7 part 2: `GetGameMediaInfoAsync` (a game's media rows: kind, file and size) and `GetMetadataEditAsync` (its title and metadata with the scraped values and the user's overrides apart), for the game options panel; `MetadataInput` checks and normalises what the user types (one line and a length; release dates as a year, month and year, or day, day first, stored as ISO 8601; ratings out of 5, out of another number, or a percentage, stored 0 to 1). <br>From 2026-10-03, `PlanDeleteAsync` and `DeleteGameAsync` (`GameDeleter`) delete a game's files and take it out of the library (A4 Deleting a game). From 2026-10-03, `GetGamesAsync` takes a `GamesOrdering` (`[display] games_sort`, or the system's own; A4 Grid queries), and `SystemOrder` sorts the systems by `[display] systems_sort` (A5) for the grid. |
| `Files` | (M7) What the pickers list, without Godot. `DirectoryListing` lists one folder on the caller's thread (the pickers use a dedicated one): folders first, then files, each in natural order ignoring case (`NaturalComparer`), filtered by a `FileFilter` (images, `.glb` models, programs), hidden and system entries skipped, with a 256 KB buffer, and a count others can read while it runs. It never throws for a folder it can't read: a missing folder, a refused one, a drive with no disc and an unreachable share come back as a reason written for the user. `PathInput` reads a typed path (quotes dropped, `/` as `\`, `C:`, `~`, relative to the folder shown, `\\server\share`) and finds the folder above, stopping at a drive's or share's root. `LetterJump` groups a list by first letter. `PickerHistory` keeps each picker use's last folder in `DataDir/picker-locations.json`. <br>`Volumes` (2026-11-02) says whether two folders are on one volume, so a move between them is a rename: by their roots on Windows; elsewhere, where every root is `/`, by the mount point that holds each (the longest from the mount table, which touches no drive; a bind mount counts as a volume of its own). The media folder's move and the theme downloader's use it. |
| `Updates` | (2026-10-18) "Check for updates" (decisions log, 2026-10-18). `ReleaseFeed` reads GitHub's releases list (`GET /repos/GeekRhapsody/OdysseyLauncher/releases`, without credentials: 60 requests an hour per address, and a check is one) into the releases with a package for this platform (`OdysseyLauncher-<version>-<platform>.zip`, as the release workflow names it; drafts and tags that aren't versions left out), and picks the newest one newer than the running launcher (`AppVersion`; pre-releases only for a pre-release); `Summary` turns the release's generated Markdown notes into a few plain lines. `IReleaseSource` is `GitHubReleaseSource` (30 s for an answer; every failure an `UpdateException` written for the user: offline, the rate limit and when it resets, a refusal) or `LocalReleaseSource` (`--update-feed`: a folder's `releases.json`, in GitHub's shape, and the files it names). `UpdateService` checks, then downloads the package into `<exe dir>/.odyssey-update/` (an earlier try's deleted first, and at every check), streaming with a 30 s stall timeout; checks its SHA-256 against the asset's `digest` (GitHub's, since 2025) or the release's `.sha256` file, and refuses a release with neither; unpacks it, refusing the whole zip for an entry outside its folder; and checks the result holds `OdysseyLauncher.exe`, its `.pck` and the installer's helper (`OdysseyUpdater.exe`, so the next update can be installed too), and none of what an update keeps (`portable.txt`, `userdata`, `override.cfg`). Nothing installed changes until the platform's `IUpdateInstaller` runs, after the launcher has quit. From 2026-11-05 (L5) Linux updates too: the installer says what its package is (`UpdatePackage`: `Zip`, or `AppImage`, `OdysseyLauncher-<version>-linux-x86_64.AppImage`, picked by that exact name as the zip is, with its `.sha256`) and which file an update replaces (`Target`: the executable, or `$APPIMAGE`); an AppImage is downloaded into `.odyssey-update/` beside the `.AppImage` file, checked against its SHA-256 and its magic (ELF, then `AI` 2), and not unpacked. |
| `Platform` | The only OS-specific code. <br>Interfaces: `IPlatformPaths`, `IProcessRunner`, `IWindowFocus`, `IFileLocations` (M7), `IPowerControl` (the power menu), `ISteamClient` (Steam games), `IDeviceStatus` (the status indicators: the battery and the network), `IUpdateInstaller` (2026-10-18: installs a downloaded update once the launcher has quit; 2026-11-05, its package's kind and the file it replaces); `PlatformServices` picks the implementations. <br>`ToolProcess` (2026-10-18), for build tooling (the export addon's publish of the update helper): runs a tool to the end with its output captured and, on Windows, in a job object of its own, so whatever it leaves running is ended when it exits (decisions log, 2026-10-18, the export that waited 15 minutes). <br>`DeviceStatusMonitor` (2026-10-02) polls `IDeviceStatus` on the thread pool every 10 s, and at once when .NET's `NetworkChange` says an address changed, and raises `Changed` only when the snapshot differs (the Wi-Fi signal compared as bars, 0 to 3, not its raw quality); it's paused while a game runs. <br>`RenderingOverride` (2026-10-08) writes and removes the driver line in Godot's `override.cfg` beside the executable, keeping any other lines (A3 Rendering). <br>**Windows** (`Platform/Windows`, `LibraryImport` to kernel32, user32, advapi32 and powrprof): <br>- `WindowsProcessRunner` creates the emulator inside a new job object (`PROC_THREAD_ATTRIBUTE_JOB_LIST`) with `CreateProcessW`, and the game has ended when the job has no processes, so stub launchers are followed. A dedicated thread waits on the job's completion port, and also asks the job every second, because Windows doesn't guarantee job messages. The job doesn't kill the game if the launcher dies, allows explicit breakaway, and no handles are inherited. <br>- A `RunFile` plan (a game that is its own program, script or shortcut) never goes through a command line cmd.exe could split: `.exe` and `.com` files run directly; `.bat` and `.cmd` files as `cmd.exe /d /s /c ""path""` (Windows starts cmd.exe itself for a batch file given to `CreateProcess`, and a name like `Sonic & Knuckles.bat` then runs `Knuckles.bat` as a second command, which a test showed), refusing a path with a `%`, which cmd would expand; anything else (a `.lnk` shortcut, a `.url`) by `ShellExecuteEx` on a COM-initialised thread, whose process is put in the game's job once it exists (a little it starts first isn't followed; a process that can't be put in a job is followed by its handle; no process at all, such as a document handed to a program already running, counts as ended at once). <br>- `WindowsCommandLine` quotes each argument by the C runtime's rules, since Windows passes one UTF-16 string. <br>- `WindowsWindowFocus`: see Platform glue below. <br>- `WicImageDecoder` (M4) decodes PNG, JPEG and WebP and scales them with the Windows Imaging Component, through raw COM vtable calls (no interop package). <br>- `MediaFoundationVideoDecoder` (2026-10-02) decodes MP4 videos with Media Foundation, the same way (`MediaFoundation` holds the vtable slots, GUIDs and calls): a source reader per stream, the picture through Media Foundation's advanced video processor to 32-bit RGB (then RGBA, cropped to the picture's aperture, top row first whatever the stride), the sound to 32-bit float. The first frame is read when the file opens, since the decoder settles the format with it. Windows' H.264 decoder takes 4:2:0 only: a 4:4:4 file (a fifth of the owner's ScreenScraper arcade videos) fails, and the reader says so, from the `avcC` box's profile. Windows N editions lack Media Foundation, which is reported as such. <br>- `WindowsPdfRenderer` (2026-10-27) renders manuals with Windows.Data.Pdf, the same way: the file opened as a WinRT stream (`CreateRandomAccessStreamOnFile`, no `StorageFile`) and loaded as a `PdfDocument`, its asynchronous calls waited for by polling their `IAsyncInfo` on the calling thread; each page drawn by its native renderer (`PdfCreateRenderer`, `IPdfRendererNative.RenderPageToSurface`) onto a Direct3D 11 surface on a WARP (software) device the reader makes and frees itself, then copied to a staging texture and read back as RGBA. Each reader initialises WinRT on its thread and uninitialises it when it's disposed. Windows.Data.Pdf's own `RenderToStreamAsync` isn't used: it draws on a hardware device it keeps for the process's life, and beside Godot's Direct3D 12 renderer the process then crashed as it exited (exception 0x87A; not on Vulkan). <br>- `WindowsFileLocations` (M7): drive letters and types (`GetLogicalDrives`, `GetDriveTypeW`) and a mapped drive's share and whether it's connected (`WNetGetConnectionW`), none of which touches the drive; volume labels separately (`GetVolumeInformationW` can block for tens of seconds on a disconnected share, so the pickers call it on a worker and don't wait); Downloads from its known folder. <br>- `WindowsPowerControl`: restart and shutdown with `ExitWindowsEx` (planned, closing hung apps; a full shutdown, not Fast Startup's hybrid), sleep with `SetSuspendState`, each after enabling the shutdown privilege in the process's token (`AdjustTokenPrivileges`). Sleep is offered only where `GetPwrCapabilities` reports S1–S3: a Modern Standby PC can't be put to sleep by an app. <br>- `WindowsDeviceStatus` (2026-10-02): the battery from `GetSystemPowerStatus` (none when `BatteryFlag` says so; plugged in from `ACLineStatus`); the network from the interface table (`GetIfTable2`): the default route's interface (`GetBestInterface` for a public address, which only reads the routing table) if it's a connected hardware adapter (Ethernet or 802.11, up, its medium connected), else the first connected Wi-Fi adapter, else the first connected Ethernet one, so a VPN or a Hyper-V switch over the real adapter still shows the real link; a Wi-Fi connection's signal quality from the WLAN service (`WlanQueryInterface`, current connection), unknown without it. Native memory only, nothing managed allocated per read. <br>- `WindowsSteamClient`: the Steam client's state from what it writes to `HKCU\Software\Valve\Steam` (`SteamExe`; `ActiveProcess\pid`, checked alive; `RunningAppID`; `Apps\<id>`'s `Installed`, `Running` and `Updating`), read with `Microsoft.Win32.Registry`; no network, no Steam API. A `Detached` plan is opened by `ShellExecuteEx` and its process handle closed at once. <br>- `WindowsUpdateInstaller` (2026-10-18): runs `OdysseyUpdater.exe` (`src/Launcher.Updater`, below), the helper the release ships beside the launcher. It copies the installed helper into the staging folder and runs it from there, so it never runs from a file it replaces, and it's the installed launcher's own, so the two always agree on its command line: the handover as options (`--pid`, `--install`, `--package`, `--backup`, `--staging`, `--exe`, `--cwd`, `--log`, `--attempts`), then `--` and the launcher's own arguments, quoted by `WindowsCommandLine`. It's started with `CreateProcessW`: no window, no inherited handles (an inherited handle to the PCK would keep it open after the launcher quits), and out of the launcher's job where the job allows breakaway. A launcher with no helper beside it says so and isn't updated. <br>**Linux** (the AppImage, 2026-11-02): `PlatformPaths` gives the XDG folders, and in an AppImage looks for `portable.txt` beside the `.AppImage` file, not in its read-only mount (A4); `PortableProcessRunner` (`Process` with `ArgumentList`; from 2026-11-04 the game runs in a process group of its own, made by util-linux's `setsid`, which executes the program in its place (the same pid and exit code), and has ended once the group is empty (`kill(-pgid, 0)` once a second after the process started ends), so a stub launcher is followed as a job follows it on Windows, and ending a game kills the group; a program that makes a session of its own escapes it, as a breakaway escapes a job; since `setsid` would start a program it can't run and exit with 127 or 126, the program is checked first (found, and executable), failing the launch as the OS would; without `setsid`, only the process started is followed; the emulator isn't given the variables an AppImage's runtime sets for the launcher: `APPIMAGE`, `APPDIR`, `ARGV0`, `OWD`); `LinuxUpdateInstaller` (2026-11-05, L5: renames the downloaded `.AppImage` over `$APPIMAGE`, keeping its name and mode, then a bash started just before it waits for the launcher's process to end, removes the empty staging folder, closes every file it inherited but the standard three (the AppImage runtime's mount point, fd 1023, which otherwise kept the old version mounted while the new one ran) and executes the new AppImage with the launcher's arguments and working folder, without the runtime's variables; if the rename fails the bash is killed, so nothing changes; a launcher not run from an AppImage says so); `NullUpdateInstaller` (any other OS); `NullWindowFocus` (2026-11-04: the owner's VMs, KDE on CachyOS and Cinnamon on Mint, have had the launcher back in front after every game with every emulator tried, so nothing asks for the foreground on Linux; Godot's `WindowMoveToForeground` was left out, being an app asking for itself, which is what focus-stealing prevention refuses; a restored minimised window is the controller's, on every OS); `LinuxPowerControl` (2026-11-04; `Platform/Linux`, `LibraryImport` to libsystemd's sd-bus, its non-variadic calls only, since .NET can't call a variadic C function on Linux): restart, shutdown and sleep through logind (`org.freedesktop.login1.Manager`'s `Reboot`, `PowerOff` and `Suspend` on the system bus, never interactive), each offered only where its `CanReboot`, `CanPowerOff` or `CanSuspend` answers "yes" ("challenge", a password logind would ask for, isn't offered; nor is anything without libsystemd). The answers are asked once, on a worker in the app's warm-up (View does nothing until then), so the menu opens with no I/O. The desktop's session manager isn't asked, so apps are sent SIGTERM as the session ends; sleep returns once logind accepts it, before the computer sleeps; `LinuxSteamClient` (L3b, 2026-11-04), the keys `WindowsSteamClient` reads, from `registry.vdf`, the text file (Valve's KeyValues) Steam keeps its registry in off Windows: `Registry/HKLM/Software/Valve/Steam/SteamPID`, checked alive in `/proc`, for `ActiveProcess\pid`; `Registry/HKCU/Software/Valve/Steam`'s `RunningAppID` and `Apps/<id>`'s `installed`, `Running` and `Updating`; keys matched ignoring case, as KeyValues are; the file is `~/.steam/registry.vdf` (Steam's own package, Arch's `steam` and Debian's `steam-installer` alike) or `~/.var/app/com.valvesoftware.Steam/.steam/registry.vdf` (Flathub's), the one written last if both are there, and `ClientPath` is that file; read whole at each poll, a file that isn't KeyValues (one caught half written) counting as nothing running. Linux's Steam has left `RunningAppID` at 0 since its 2023 interface (Valve's steam-for-linux issue 9672), so there the app's own `Running` flag decides, trusted once seen clear as on Windows; Flathub's `SteamPID` is likely the sandbox's own pid, which means nothing outside it (the owner's manual test). No image decoder in Core, so the app gives Core Godot's (`Textures/GodotImageDecoder`), which bakes the themes' logos; `FfmpegVideoDecoder` (L4, 2026-11-04), videos decoded by the FFmpeg the AppImage carries (decisions log, 2026-11-04): FFmpeg 9.0, LGPL, built by `tools/linux/setup.sh` from its pinned source tarball with only the H.264 (its 4:4:4 profiles too), HEVC, VP9, AAC, MP3 and Opus decoders and the MP4 demuxer, as shared libraries in the AppImage's `usr/lib`. `BundledLibraries` loads them from beside the executable, its `../lib` or beside Launcher.Core (the tests), never the system's: the bindings (`FfmpegNativeMethods`, `LibraryImport` by hand) name each library `odyssey-<file>`, which no system has, and the five are loaded by full path in dependency order, so the dynamic loader finds each one's dependencies among those loaded (by soname) without searching. Structs FFmpeg gives no accessors for are read at offsets (`FfmpegNativeMethods.Abi`) that `FfmpegAbiTests` compares with the headers' (`tools/linux/ffmpeg-abi.c`, run by `setup.sh`). As Media Foundation does: each stream a reader of its own that opens the file, the first frame decoded as it opens, the picture converted by swscale to RGBA straight into the caller's buffer (FFmpeg crops it; a picture that doesn't name its colourspace is BT.709 from 720 lines up, else BT.601), the sound by swresample to interleaved float at its own rate; at most 4 decoding threads a video; a damaged packet skipped. The libraries are loaded with the first video opened, on its worker, so creating the decoder does no I/O. `PdfiumRenderer` (L4, 2026-11-04), manuals rendered by the PDFium the AppImage carries (decisions log, 2026-11-04): pdfium-binaries' build, release `chromium/8138` (no V8 or XFA; glibc 2.16), `libpdfium.so` in `usr/lib`, loaded as FFmpeg is (`PdfiumNativeMethods`), with its licences and those of the libraries built into it in `usr/share/licenses/pdfium`. As Windows.Data.Pdf does: page sizes in device-independent pixels, turned by the page's `/Rotate` (PDFium's own sizes are), and each page drawn whole on white paper, scaled to the size asked for, straight into the caller's buffer as RGBA (a BGRA bitmap over that buffer, its bytes reversed by `FPDF_REVERSE_BYTE_ORDER`), with its annotations; the page last drawn is kept. PDFium isn't thread-safe, so every call into it takes one lock: the viewer and the thumbnails' readers take turns, where Windows renders them at once; `LinuxFileLocations` (2026-11-04: `/`, then what's mounted under `/run/media/<user>` (udisks2 on Arch and its kin), `/media/<user>` (udisks2 on Debian and its kin) and `/mnt`, from `/proc/self/mountinfo`, which touches no drive; each named after its folder, a share (CIFS, NFS, SSHFS) with its source as the detail; a drive that isn't mounted isn't listed; and the home folder, Desktop and Downloads); `LinuxDeviceStatus` (2026-11-04), the kernel's files as `WindowsDeviceStatus` reads Windows' APIs: the battery is the first system battery in `/sys/class/power_supply` (`type` Battery, its `scope` not Device, which is a pad's), plugged in when a Mains or USB supply is online, or, with none listed, when the battery isn't discharging; the connection is the default route's interface (`/proc/net/route`, the lowest metric) if it's a connected hardware adapter (a `device` link, `operstate` up), else the first connected Wi-Fi one (`wireless` or `phy80211`), else the first other one; the Wi-Fi signal is `/proc/net/wireless`'s level in dBm, made 0–100 as NetworkManager does (2 × (dBm + 100)). .NET's interface list isn't used: it has no signal, and no idea which adapter carries the default route. No rendering override: Vulkan is the Mobile renderer's only driver there (A3 Rendering). |
| `Launcher.Updater` | (2026-10-18; its own project, `src/Launcher.Updater`, not part of Core, so the helper has none of Core's dependencies) `OdysseyUpdater.exe`, a Windows program with no console, published with Native AOT (about 1.8 MB, no .NET needed) by the export addon (`godot/addons/odyssey_export/UpdaterExportPlugin`, through `Platform/ToolProcess`) and put beside the launcher. It waits up to a minute for the launcher's process (an id that's now another program's isn't waited for), then, for each of the package's top-level files and folders, renames the installed one into the staging folder's `old/` and the new one into its place, trying each rename 20 times half a second apart (an antivirus scanner or the indexer can hold a file a moment after the launcher quits); if one fails, it renames everything back. Renames only (`File.Move`, `Directory.Move`), all within the launcher's folder, on one volume: a rename happens whole or not at all. Then it starts the launcher (the new version, or the old one after a failure) with the arguments after `--`, logs each step to `DataDir/logs/update.log`, and deletes the staging folder but itself (it can't delete the program it is; the launcher deletes what's left at its next check). If something couldn't be put back, the old files wait in `old/` with an `incomplete.txt`, and `UpdateService` neither deletes the folder nor updates again until the user has dealt with it. Everything else in the launcher's folder stays: `portable.txt` and `userdata` (a portable install's data), `override.cfg`, and anything the user put there. The app's own themes (`themes/`) are replaced whole, so edits made there are lost: themes of the user's own belong in `ConfigDir/themes/`. Exit codes: 0 installed, 1 not, 2 bad arguments. |
| `Diagnostics` | Debug-argument parser, `StartupTimeline`, `FrameTimeStats`, `BenchReport` (System.Text.Json source generation), a minimal `ILog`, and `Redactor` (M4), which masks every known credential (as written, URL-encoded and JSON-escaped) and the value of any credential-bearing URL parameter or bearer header. |

Every `await` in Core uses `ConfigureAwait(false)`. Analyser rule CA2007 is an error in `src/`.

### Godot app (`godot/src`, namespace `Launcher.App.*`)

| Area | Responsibility |
|---|---|
| `Boot` | Main scene (M5). `AppServices` loads config, resolves the theme (M6: manifests and each system's model candidates) and opens the library on the thread pool. The main thread makes the cover array while that runs, and starts loading the theme's models as soon as the theme is resolved, while the DB opens (from 2026-10-27 its games' templates and the virtual cards; the systems' cards once the library says which systems the grid shows). When both are done it builds the look and the grids and binds the systems grid; its first drawn frame is `interactive`. A headless run (`--launch` only) resolves no theme. A warm-up follows, one step a frame: the settings screen, the launch controller, glyphs, then the status indicators (2026-10-02: their first uses, the time zone, the time format, the icons and the device monitor, on the thread pool; only their nodes on the main thread) and the navigation sounds (2026-10-10: loaded on the thread pool, then their node made and given to the navigator). Systems never scanned are scanned in the background after it, and then covers without a derivative are baked (M4; also after F5). `MainThreadQueue` carries every background result to the main thread within 3 ms a frame. `GodotWork` (2026-10-11) counts background work that calls into Godot (models, thumbnails, videos): as the app quits, `Main._ExitTree` refuses new work and waits for the rest, so nothing calls the engine as it shuts down. |
| `Screens` | `Navigator` (M5): Systems → Games → Launching → back. Transitions move the two grids in depth, scale them and fade them into the background, staggered so the opaque grids barely overlap. From M6: entering a system cross-fades to its look; the next theme (T, or the one chosen in the settings from M7) loads in the background and is applied without a restart (templates, slot layout, look, and the shown list loaded again with the new theme's media kinds); per-game models load when their list does and replace their game's template in place; `MediaChanged` re-reads the shown list's media and rebinds only the games whose media changed. The last focused game is remembered per system, and the last 3 game lists are cached. Favourites, Recently played and All games (2026-10-15) are cards in the systems grid, mixing templates. A system with no games has no card (`[display] hide_empty_systems`, on by default, A5), so the built-in catalogue shows only what the user has; the grid is built again after each rescan, and a system never scanned is scanned in the background after boot. A game's details are queried on the thread pool 0.12 s after the focus rests, so held moves don't query; from 2026-11-11 whether it's a favourite is its list row's, shown with its title, and a details read that finds it changed (L3, or the details screen, both reading them again) corrects the row in the shown and cached lists. From 2026-11-02, `GridLabels` (one for each grid, in the overlay under the scrims) draws the grid with labels' titles: a `Label` per pool cell, two lines a column wide at most, its top middle where `ItemGrid.TryGetLabel` says, scaled and faded with its grid, set only when `LabelsVersion` changes, after the grids tick (`Main._Process`, so they never trail the items); the games' labels go behind the 3D with the list's titles while a game is inspected (`InfoOverlay.TitlesBehind`, `ListBehind` until then). `InfoOverlay` is the PS2-style text: the title with a soft glow top left, what it belongs to under it (a system's maker, year and games; a game's system, then for a favourite ` · ` and "Favourite" in gold: 2026-11-11, until then "Favourite" was top right under the status indicators), and controls bottom right (2026-10-02: a game's metadata moved to its details screen; 2026-10-03: a system's too, below). <br>From M7: Menu, or B or Escape on the systems screen, opens the settings, and the navigator ignores input until they close and every button is let go; rescans (F5, and the settings) run through `LibraryJobs` with a progress card; config the settings save rebuilds the systems grid. <br>From M7 part 2: X opens the focused system's or game's options; Favourites' and All games' are their view and order (2026-11-10: `VirtualListPage`, which shares a system's "Games view" rows through `GamesViewPanel`), and Recently played has none. Games whose title or metadata changed (a scrape, a clear, an edit: `LibraryJobs.GamesUpdated`) have the shown list read again 0.4 s later, several changes being read once: titles are updated in place (`GamesSource.UpdateTitles`, `RefreshItem`) when the games are still in the same order, else the list is bound again with the focus on the same game; the focused game's details are read again at once. A system's own models or template choice changing reloads the theme (`ReloadTheme`, a switch to the same theme). <br>View (Select) or P in either grid opens `PowerMenu`: Restart, Shut down and Sleep through `IPowerControl`, on a worker (sleep returns only on waking), and Quit Odyssey Launcher (the four were Restart system, Shut down system, Sleep system and Quit app until 2026-11-07). A row acts at once; B or View again closes it. A restart or shutdown Windows accepted quits the app, so the databases close before Windows ends it; a refusal is shown in a dialog. <br>From 2026-10-02, `StatusBar`: the status indicators top right (the network, the battery and the time, each on or off by `[ui]`, A5), on a canvas layer of their own above the settings screens, so a change made there shows at once (only `--no-overlay` hides them). The row is an `HBoxContainer` anchored top right and growing leftwards, which leaves hidden indicators out of its layout, so whatever is shown sits together against the right edge. The icons are [Lucide](https://lucide.dev)'s (`godot/icons/status/`, ISC), imported at 4× with mipmaps and loaded on the thread pool; a Wi-Fi signal below full draws every arc faintly with its bars over them; a battery at 10% or less is red, one charging green. The device's state comes from `DeviceStatusMonitor` through the main-thread queue, only on a change; the clock is checked once a second in `Tick` (no allocation) and its text, in the user's regional short-time format, made only when the minute changes. The overlay's title stops short of them, and the job cards sit under them. <br>From 2026-10-02, **Y on a game opens `GameDetailsPanel`**, on the settings layer: every field the library has for it (`DetailsFormatter.GameFields`: release, genre, developer, publisher, players, rating; region, languages, version, disc and other tags; play count, last played and play time; favourite, emulator, whose title and metadata, when it was scraped, size and file) in two columns under its whole description, in one block that up and down scroll; and a card for its video, its manual (2026-10-27), its front cover and its screenshot, then each other image in slot order, thumbnails decoded on a worker one after another (a video's from a frame a little way in that isn't dark, a manual's its first page). A on a card opens `MediaViewer`, a full-screen panel on black (no header; the status indicators step aside for it): an image decoded on a worker to fit the window's larger side, or the video through `Ui/VideoPlayback`; left and right go to the previous and next, A pauses and plays (and plays again once ended), and the caption (what it is, which of how many, the controls, a video's progress) fades after 3 s. L3 (or F) toggles the favourite there as in the grid, and the overlay reads it again. `--open=details` opens it for `--start-system`'s game at `--start-index`. <br>From 2026-10-27, A on the manual's card opens **`ManualViewer`** instead, full screen on black: a page at a time through `Ui/ManualPages`, fitted to the window. Left and right (or up and down) turn the page, the shoulders ten pages, the triggers (or Home and End) go to the first and last; A or R3 zooms the page to the window's width, where up and down, the right stick (or W and S) and the mouse wheel scroll it, and scrolling on past its end turns the page. Until a page's render comes, the last render of it shows (zoomed, its whole page scaled up). The caption (Manual, the page of how many, the controls) fades after 3 s. Its `Tick` allocates nothing, and it keeps the texture it shows itself rather than reading it back from its `TextureRect`: a texture `ManualPages` has freed, read back, comes with a new wrapper holding it, which kept a zoomed page's 21 MB until a collection. Off Windows there's no renderer yet, and the card and the viewer say so. `--open=manual` opens it for `--start-system`'s game at `--start-index`. <br>From 2026-10-03, **Y on a system (or Favourites, Recently played or All games) opens `SystemDetailsPanel`**, the same way: formatted on the thread pool (`DetailsFormatter.SystemFields`: maker, year, games and last scan; the emulator and its alternatives; the ROM folders, whether subfolders are scanned, what's left out, the file types and aliases; the games' view, grid size and sort, marked when the system has its own; the card's and games' models in use, as the system's options name them; its ScreenScraper and IGDB ids, the Steam store, and its id; Favourites, Recently played and All games: their description, and their games and systems counted), then pushed on the settings layer. Its card's model stands large on the left in `ModelView`: a `SubViewport` with a world of its own (`OwnWorld3D`, so its environment and lights don't touch the grids'), the grids' camera, the system's look through a `LookStage` of its own (gradient, ambient, lights), and a one-cell `ItemGrid` in the one-at-a-time layout, focused, and zoomed so the model fills about two thirds of the view (`ItemScale`), so it's drawn exactly as in the grid (tint, generated label and title, clips, the focus's sway, and from 2026-10-27 its card's video: A6 Card videos) with nothing new in the shader. It renders at the grids' pixel density (the window's pixels per UI unit times the main view's 3D scale: at most 1080p), every frame while the panel is open. There's one, made on first use and kept: when the panel closes it's parked under the settings layer, hidden, its render target not updated and its grid unbound, and the next details screen takes it with another model (making it on every open cost 10–15 ms of the main thread and a longer render). The right stick (or W, A, S and D) turns it (`ItemGrid.Turn`), and left and right turn it a step (0.3 rad); up and down scroll the description and fields beside it; Y or B goes back. <br>From 2026-10-02 the grids take their layouts from `[display]` (and a system's own games layout and grid size), applied on each bind and again when config is saved (the games shown are bound again only if their layout changed); `--layout` overrides them for a run. The list layout's titles are `TitleList`, part of the overlay (so it hides and dims with it), under the heading on the left: a pool of labels re-bound as titles scroll into view, following the games grid's own scroll (a line per title), so the list and the model beside it move together with nothing to keep in step; the focused title stays a third of the way down, under a highlight, and Favourites, Recently played and All games add each game's system. LB and RB page by the titles shown. <br>From 2026-10-03 the systems follow `[display] systems_sort` (A5), after Favourites, Recently played and All games, which always come first, and a system's games follow its `games_sort` (`DisplaySettings.GamesSortFor`). Each list remembers the order it was read in (`GamesSource.Ordering`), so a cached list in another order isn't used; when the order is changed in the settings the shown list is read again, the focus staying on the same game; and a list sorted by plays (last played, time played) is read again shortly after a game ends, and cached ones are dropped. Letter jumps still jump where the first letter changes, which suits only title order. |
| `Navigation` | `NavInput` (M5) polls `nav_*` actions each frame rather than using `_Input`, which allocates per event: arrows, Enter/Space, Escape/Backspace, Page Up/Down, Home/End, `[` `]`, F and F5; the D-pad, left stick, A/B/Y, shoulders (page), triggers (letter) and View (rescan; from 2026-10-01 the power menu, with P on the keyboard, and F5 alone rescans; from 2026-10-18 F5 is "Rescan the library" and Shift+F5 "Rescan the library and media", whose action comes first in the presses because an action matches a key with more modifiers than its own). A held move repeats after 0.32 s, then speeds up from every 120 ms to every 35 ms over 1.4 s. Input is dropped while `LaunchController.IsInputBlocked`, and a button still held when that ends doesn't count. <br>From M7 each command is two actions, `nav_*` (keyboard) and `nav_*_pad` (gamepad), so a screen that takes typing (the on-screen keyboard) polls the pad alone; X (`Alternate`: from part 2, also O or the menu key, and an item's options in the grids) and Menu or F1 (`Menu`: the settings; from 2026-11-07, in a system's games, that system's page) are added (from 2026-10-02, Y or I is `Secondary`: a game's details in the grid (from 2026-10-03 a system's too), a list's second action in the settings; L3 or F is `Favourite`, in the grid and the details screen), T alone switches theme, and Godot's own `ui_*` navigation actions are emptied at boot, so the settings screens' focus moves only from these commands, with the same repeat. <br>From 2026-10-03 the right stick turns the focused item. It isn't a command: its four directions are gamepad actions (`nav_turn_*_pad`, a 0.2 deadzone), read each frame by `NavInput.ReadTurn` (`Input.GetVector`: a round deadzone, nothing allocated), and while neither a game nor a settings screen holds the input, the navigator hands it to the shown grid's `Turn`. <br>From 2026-10-06, R3 (pressing the right stick) or Z is `Inspect`: in the games grid, the focused game large in the middle of the view (`ItemGrid.ToggleInspect`), and back. B puts an inspected game back before it leaves the system; moving the focus puts it back too. |
| `Ui` | (M7) The settings screens' toolkit: Godot controls driven by `NavInput`. `UiLayer` (a canvas layer over the dimmed grid) stacks `UiPanel`s and polls the commands each frame while one is open (and does nothing while none is): the top panel takes a command first, then the layer moves the focus with `Control.FindValidFocusNeighbor`, presses the focused button, or goes back. Panels under the top one can't take focus or clicks, and a page hides the page below it (a dialog dims it). The mouse works through Godot's GUI events: buttons and the wheel. From 2026-10-08 there's no mouse-only control: no Back or Cancel button in a header or the media viewer, and no Cancel on a job's card (B and Escape go back; Library cancels a job). `UiStyle` builds the theme in code; from 2026-10-15 its colours are the active theme's `MenuPalette` and its font the theme's menu font (A6 Menus): `Apply` sets the shared style boxes and label settings (its own, and those other classes `Keep`) to the new palette, silently and then one `changed` each (the Godot `Theme` holding its own until they all have), and walks the menus shown (the UI layer and the job cards, `Follow`) once, mapping each colour a label, frame or dimming rectangle copied to its role's new colour; `Ui/MenuLook` applies a theme's menus (at the warm-up's settings step, and in `Navigator.ApplyTheme`) and loads its font on the thread pool (`FontFile.LoadDynamicFont`, under `GodotWork`), then draws its printable ASCII at the menus' three main sizes, a part a frame. Components: `SettingRow` and `ListPanel` (rows, some adjusted with left and right, and from 2026-10-07 a picture before a row's text (`ShowPicture`, `SetPicture`: a search hit's cover); from 2026-10-07 the list scrolls to the focused row itself, at the end of the frame, from its place in the list: Godot's follow-focus overshot when several moves came in one frame; from 2026-10-10 also when the list's height changes, as when the status line under it comes or goes, which left a row at the bottom half hidden); `ChoicePanel`; `ConfirmDialog` (also one-button messages; the safe answer focused for an action that can't be undone); `OnScreenKeyboard` over `TextField` (letters, then symbols and accented letters; the pad as on the Xbox keyboard; a physical keyboard types straight in through `_Input` while it's on top; hidden entry with Show for credentials; validation before Done); `FilePicker` over `VirtualList` (folder or file mode, a filter, the places and drives at the top level with labels read on workers, a typed path, a thumbnail decoded and scaled on a worker by the OS decoder, each use's last folder, and a folder that's slow to open left behind with B); `BackgroundJobs` (a job's progress written from any thread, one refresh a frame at most) and `JobsHud` (a card per job, top right, with no buttons; hidden while the settings are open). <br>From 2026-10-02: a panel can be `PanelPlacement.FullScreen` (the whole window on black, no header or hints; `UiLayer.TopChanged` lets the status indicators hide under it); `VideoPlayback` plays an MP4 (Godot plays only Ogg Theora) into an `ImageTexture` with its sound through an `AudioStreamGenerator`: a thread of its own decodes 4 frames ahead, each into an `Image` of its own (`SetData` off the main thread; the texture made there too), and about a second of sound into a ring; once a frame the main thread feeds the generator, takes the time from the sound actually played (frames pushed, less what the generator still holds, less the output latency; the frame time when there's no sound, or once it has played out), and uploads the latest frame due. Nothing it does a frame allocates, and it never waits for its thread, which lets its readers go itself. <br>From 2026-10-27, `ManualPages`: a manual's pages as textures, for `ManualViewer`. A thread of its own (below normal, holding `GodotWork` for its run and seeing `Quitting` within 50 ms) opens the PDF through Core's `IPdfRenderer` and renders the pages asked for, newest request first, each into an `Image` with its texture made there. The viewer asks for the page it shows, whole or zoomed, and gets its two neighbours rendered whole after it, so a page turn is usually there already; only those are kept and the rest freed, and a page already rendering or on its way to the main thread isn't asked for again. A whole page fits the window (at most 2160 high); a zoomed one fills its width (rendered at most 2560 wide; the screen scales it the rest). At 1080p on the owner's machine a page of a made-up manual took 7–40 ms fitted and 53–66 ms zoomed, plus 1–24 ms for its texture. Its textures go as the viewer closes; Godot's Direct3D 12 allocator keeps a block of about one and a half times the largest page's texture for reuse (about 30 MB after a zoomed 1080p page), level however many pages are read. <br>From 2026-10-10, `UiSounds`: the navigation sounds, while `[ui] navigation_sounds` is on (the default; read at each play, so a change applies at once): a tock when the navigator's `Run` moves the focus to another system or game (a move, a page, first or last, a letter jump; a move blocked at an edge is silent), and a whoosh when a system (or Favourites, Recently played or All games) is entered. Nothing else sounds: leaving a system, launching and the settings screens are silent. The files are `godot/sounds/nav_tock.wav` and `nav_whoosh.wav` (48 kHz mono PCM, imported uncompressed into the PCK), synthesised by `tools/ui-sounds/make_sounds.py`. Two `AudioStreamPlayer`s on the master bus, which is muted while a game runs: the tock's has 4 voices, so a held move's repeats (every 35 ms at the fastest) overlap rather than cut each other off, and plays through an `AudioStreamRandomizer` that varies each play's pitch by up to 3%, so a long scroll doesn't drone. Playing allocates nothing. |
| `Settings` | (M7) The main settings screen. `SettingsController` opens it and saves: `ConfigWriter.Save` on the thread pool, after a check that may need I/O (the folder exists), then on the main thread the new config goes to `AppServices` (the library, launcher and navigator read it), a chosen theme is switched to, and new credentials or scraping settings retire the scrape service once no scrape uses it. Pages (a short list since 2026-11-07, decisions log): `SettingsHome` (a row per page with a one-line summary; above them, only while there are any, a row per running job, A cancelling it, the focus starting on Library below them), `LibraryPage` (the ROM root, the media folder, the two rescans and "Rescan when the launcher starts", "Auto scan the library at launch" until 2026-11-07), `SystemsPage` (every system, those with games first with their counts, each opening its `SystemPage`), `AppearancePage` (the theme and "Download themes"; from 2026-10-27 "Show systems without games" and from 2026-11-03 "Group regional variants", Yes or No; then, as the Layout page until 2026-11-07, from 2026-10-02 the systems' and games' layouts and grid sizes, each a list on A and stepped with left and right, and from 2026-10-03 what each is sorted by, and which way), `InterfacePage` (the status indicators, UI size, button icons and navigation sounds), `GraphicsPage` (2026-10-08: the screen mode, the 3D resolution or the window's size, the video driver with a restart offered, and anisotropic filtering; A3 Rendering), `AboutPage` ("Check for updates", the config problems and the settings folder; the problems on `ConfigProblemsPanel`, every one as its file, line and key over its message, as wide as the window allows and scrolled with up and down, where a dialog had shown the first 12 drawn smaller to fit), `SystemPage` (each system's folders and emulator, and from 2026-10-10 "Auto-configure controllers", Yes or No, for a system offering an emulator whose controllers the launcher can set up, with (2026-10-11) "Switch mapping for A/B and X/Y" under it; a folder change rescans what it affects), `MediaFolderPage` (2026-10-04: the media folder, another folder or the default, its files moved there by `MediaMovePanel` or the folder used as it is; A4 The media folder's location), `EmulatorsPage` (from 2026-10-07 first the emulators folder and RetroArch's, the `{emulators}` and `{retroarch}` variables; then each profile's program (only the profiles systems with games offer, with empty systems hidden; a `run_file` profile has no program to pick), checked off the main thread; profiles sharing one install move together, through the variable they share when there is one), `ScrapingPage` (first "Scrape what's missing", "Scrape all missing metadata" on the main page until 2026-11-07), `FallbackPage`, `ScrapeMediaPage` (from 2026-10-01: each media kind scraping downloads, on or off, with the providers that have it), `RegionsPage` (from 2026-10-04: `[scraping] regions`, each of `ScreenScraperScraper.KnownRegions` on or off and moved earlier or later, as `FallbackPage` does, both an `OrderedListPage`: A on a row turns it on or off, and ↑ and ↓ buttons beside it, which take the focus as the row does (left and right go between them), move it, the focus following it on the same row or button; a code of the user's own keeps its place) and `ProviderPage` (credentials masked, where each is set, cleared, and tested). `LibraryJobs` runs rescans and "scrape all missing" as jobs bound to the library's progress and the scrape service's events, cancellable, and owns the scrape service. All of it is built in the warm-up after `interactive`. <br>From M7 part 2, `SystemPage` is also a system's options (X on it in the grid): (2026-10-02) its games' own view and grid columns and rows, and (2026-10-03) their sort and order (each Default, the Appearance page's, until set; Y goes back to it), its models (its card, and its games' template: one of the themes' templates as `game_model`, or the user's own `.glb`) and "scrape this system", counted and asked first; from 2026-10-04 also "scrape games without a front cover", "… without a screenshot" and "… not scraped in the last 30 days" (a `SystemScrapeFilter` each, counted and asked first the same way). `LibraryJobs` also scrapes a system (a batch, like "all missing") and a game (a job of its own, which can run beside a batch: the service puts single games first), clears a game, and raises `GamesUpdated` on the main thread as each game is done. <br>From 2026-10-16, "Download themes" (under Appearance since 2026-11-07) (A6 Downloading themes): `ThemeDownloader`, which the controller owns and makes the first time the page opens (so a download carries on when it closes, and nothing of it loads at boot), runs Core's `ThemeDownloads` with a progress card (`BackgroundJobs`, kind `theme`) and raises `ThemeUpdated` when the theme in use changed on disk, which reloads it; `ThemeDownloaderPage` lists the themes with their first screenshot and state (A gets, updates or uses one, X shows its details, Y removes it), and `ThemeDetailsPanel` shows one's description, version, what it brings and its screenshots, each full screen in `MediaViewer`. <br>From 2026-10-18, "Check for updates" (on `AboutPage` since 2026-11-07) (Core's `Updates`; the decisions log, 2026-10-18). `AppUpdater`, which the controller makes the first time About opens (so nothing of it is at boot, and the launcher never checks unasked), checks on the thread pool, then says "You have the newest version" in the status line, or offers a newer release in a question: its version and yours, the first lines of its notes, its size, that settings, library, media and themes are kept, and which running job stops. Yes pushes `UpdatePanel` over the settings (Menu doesn't close them while it works): the download's progress, a few times a second, and Stop (A or B), which deletes what came. Then it starts the installer (`OdysseyUpdater.exe`) from the worker and quits a frame after saying so, closing the databases as any quit does. A failure is told in a dialog that says nothing changed. The launcher starts again with the same command line (`OS.GetCmdlineArgs`, then `--` and the user's), so `--user-dir` survives. An editor run checks but never installs (the executable is Godot's), nor does a launcher whose executable was renamed. <br>From 2026-10-04, `SystemPage` has "Import gamelist.xml" (A4 Imported gamelists): the system's gamelist found off the main thread (several: a `ChoicePanel`, with "Choose a file…"; none: the file picker, `.xml`), read and matched, then a question saying what it adds (games in the library and not, metadata, files per kind, favourites, matches, hidden games left shown). `LibraryJobs.ImportGamelist` runs it as a job of its own (a progress card in files, cancellable; one at a time), raising `GamesUpdated` once the metadata is saved. |
| `Options` | (M7 part 2) The item options. `ItemOptions` opens them (X in the grids, through the navigator's events) on the settings layer, owns the services they use (`ModelImportService`, `UserArtService`), and passes changes on: a game's edits to `LibraryJobs.GamesUpdated`, a system's models to a theme reload. `GameOptionsPanel`: the emulator (an override, or the system's), its own model (a `.glb` or OBJ zip, Y removes it), images, title and metadata, "scrape this game" (matching is manual: `GameMatchPanel`, below; the outcome in the panel's status) and "clear metadata" (a confirmation saying what goes, including the user's own images), and last, from 2026-10-03, "delete this game" (A4 Deleting a game: the question lists every file that goes, the first six and a count, their size and folder, what's kept and why, with "Keep it" focused; once deleted the panel closes, and `LibraryJobs.GameDeleted` has the navigator build the systems grid again and read the shown list again with the focus on the next game, or go back to the systems if the list's card dropped out; a file that couldn't go is told in a dialog). `GameMetadataPanel`: each field with the user's value or the scraped one and where it came from; A types one on the on-screen keyboard, checked by `MetadataInput` before it's saved; Y goes back to the scraped value; a value equal to the scraped one isn't kept as the user's. `GameMediaPanel`: a card per media slot with its image (decoded off the main thread by `Ui/Thumbnails`, shared with the image picker), its file (from 2026-10-02 the media folder doesn't say whether it was scraped), its size and whether the system's template shows it; A chooses the user's own with the image picker, replacing the slot's file; Y removes the slot's file, scraped or not. From 2026-10-27 (the panel, and its row in the game's options, are "Media") a card for the game's video and one for its manual follow the slots, five cards a row, with their first frame or page, their length or page count, and their size; A chooses the user's own MP4 or PDF with the file picker (each with its own last folder), and Y removes it, the question saying it comes back if the game is scraped again while that kind is on in Scraping › Media: nothing remembers a removal, as for images. `GameMatchPanel` (2026-10-01): every provider's search results in a section of its own (the name, year, id and how close the name is, and from 2026-10-07 its cover on the left, so games of one name can be told apart: downloaded and decoded on workers, each provider's from the top of its list two at a time, "…" until then and "No cover" when it has none, kept while the panel is open and dropped, with what's still loading, when it closes), each provider's current match marked "In use" (one the search doesn't list gets a row of its own, with an empty frame); a provider that couldn't be searched says why; X searches for a name typed on the on-screen keyboard; A on a result scrapes the game with it, through `LibraryJobs.ScrapeGame`. `--open=match` shows it over made-up results, for captures. Every panel reads and writes off the main thread and reads again when the library says the game changed. |
| `Grid` | `ItemGrid` (M5), a virtualised 3D grid. <br>A cell pool (at most 64 for games) covers the visible rows plus spare rows, most of them ahead of the scroll, and cells are re-bound rather than recreated. <br>Template items are drawn as **one `MultiMesh` per template**, with materials of the one item shader (M6: templates share one unless their authored textures or tint differ, so there's never a variant). Each MultiMesh has an instance per pool cell, but from 2026-10-08 the cells it draws are packed into its first instances and `VisibleInstanceCount` is how many (`PlaceInstance`, `RemoveInstance`: a cell leaving takes the last one's instance), because the GPU shades every instance's vertices, hidden or not: drawing all 64 ran each template's whole mesh once per cell, about 9 million vertices a frame for the console theme's system models (11 ms of GPU on the Deck, 1.6 ms packed). From 2026-10-27 its node shows only while it has instances in use: Godot doesn't pass an empty MultiMesh's moves on, so one refilled after the grid had scrolled was drawn for a frame where the grid had been. The warm-up draws each template in a one-instance MultiMesh of its own. Each pool cell has a fixed layer per slot channel in the streamer's arrays (`SlotLayout`), a row of the slot-state texture and a fixed place in the title atlas. `INSTANCE_CUSTOM` = (the cell, the plain colour packed as a 24-bit integer, idle phase or −1 when focused, 1 when an idle clip animates it plus 2 while it's inspected): Godot 4 multiplies a MultiMesh's instance colour into `COLOR`, which carries material data here. The slot-state texture (RGBA32F, 8 slots × 64 cells, created once and only updated) holds, per cell and slot, the fallback shown without media, the media's aspect, its fade and its layer; from 2026-10-01 two more columns per cell reshape a box to its game's art (A7 Boxes shaped by the art), zero for every other model. <br>Each slot walks its template's fallback chain against the game's media when a cell binds; `RefreshItem` redoes that after the game's media changed, keeping its model and unchanged slots. <br>**Items drawn as nodes (M6 part 2).** A per-game model can't share a template's MultiMesh, so it's drawn on the cell's own `MeshInstance3D`, one per pool cell made with the grid (binding one sets its mesh and material, and allocates nothing), fitted to the cell. A model with clips is drawn on a copy of its node tree (pooled per model, made the first time a cell needs one): a template with an `idle` clip in every cell, one with only `focused` or `launch` clips only in the focused cell, which swaps back to the MultiMesh once its clip has blended to the rest pose. A node's per-cell data is the shader's `node_custom` instance uniform (a `Vector4`: Godot converts a `Color` given to one from sRGB to linear), so it's the same material and no new variant. Per-game models' materials are made when they load (`PrepareModel`), not while scrolling. <br>**The items never move to scroll: the grid's root does** (one transform), as the camera would, so a frame only touches newly bound rows, the focused items and fading covers. Two grids (systems and games) each have their own root. <br>Items are sized so 3.2 rows of games fit (2.7 of systems), unless that leaves fewer than 5 columns.
