  - Its title and metadata are written as **the user's own edits** (`game_overrides` in `userdata.db`), so a rebuild keeps them, scraping never replaces them, the game's metadata panel shows them as the user's, and clearing the game removes them. A field the game has, scraped or the user's, is kept; a title is taken only when the game has neither the user's nor a scraped one, and not when it's the file's own name (an unscraped entry's) or the cleaned file-name title. Its `sortname` becomes the game's sort title (2026-11-09) when the user hasn't given it one.
  - Its media is **copied** (the gamelist's files are only read) into the media folder as the game's own files: `thumbnail` the cover, `image` the screenshot, `marquee` the logo, `video` the video, `manual` the manual (2026-10-06); other tags (`fanart`, ...) aren't imported. A kind the game has a file for is skipped, and a file is never replaced (as a download: a temporary file, then a move that doesn't overwrite), so importing again only finishes what was left. They're ordinary media-folder files: a rebuild indexes them.
  - `<favorite>true</favorite>` makes the game a favourite (one never stops being one). `<game id>` is stored as the game's ScreenScraper match (method `gamelist`) when a `<scrap name="ScreenScraper">` says it's ScreenScraper's and the game has no ScreenScraper match, stored or manual; it's in `library.db`, so a rebuild drops it and the next scrape looks the game up again.
  - `<hidden>` isn't imported: the launcher has no way to show a hidden game again yet.
- **Orphans:** when a file disappears, its `userdata.db` rows are kept, so moving a ROM out and back loses nothing.
- **Deleting a game (2026-10-03),** from its options, deletes its file from the ROM folder, for good (not to the Recycle Bin). A folder game (2026-10-10, A1 `Scanning`) isn't deleted: the options say to delete it in Explorer, and `GameDeleter.Delete` refuses its plan (`GameDeletePlan.IsFolder`). A playlist takes every file it lists, following playlists inside it (an `.m3u` of `.cue` discs takes their tracks), resolved as the scanner resolves them (never outside the ROM folder; a playlist over 256 KB isn't read, as its files are games of their own); a file another game's playlist lists (from the `playlists` table) stays, with whatever it lists. The files are found first, read from disk (`PlanDeleteAsync`), and the user is asked about exactly those (`DeleteGameAsync` takes the plan). The game's own file goes first; if it can't, nothing else is tried and the library is untouched. Then the rest (a read-only flag is cleared: the user asked), and folders the delete left empty, up to but not including the ROM folder. The game leaves `library.db` in one transaction (its row, cascading to its metadata, media rows, matches and scrape state; the deleted playlists' rows; the system's count), without a rescan, which on a NAS lists the whole folder; it waits for a running scan, which could otherwise add the game back from a listing made before the delete. Like any file that disappears, its `userdata.db` rows and its files in the media folder stay, so adding the game again brings back its art, favourite and play history.

### `library.db` (schema version 5, `PRAGMA user_version = 5`)

The shipped files are `src/Launcher.Core/Data/Migrations/Library/0001_initial.sql`, `0002_media_file_stamps.sql` (M5: `media.size_bytes` and `mtime_ms`, the indexed file's size and time, so an unchanged file's header isn't read again, and so the grid can name its derivative without touching it; scraped media rows have them too), `0003_scraping.sql` (M4: `metadata.title`, the scraped title, and `scrape_state`, below) `0004_media_folder.sql` (2026-10-02: `media.source` dropped, and every system marked never scanned, so the app rescans them all once into the media folder's rows) and `0005_games_added.sql` (2026-10-03: `games.added_ms`, below, with every system marked never scanned again so a rescan fills it). This copy is version 1, for reading.

```sql
-- 0005 (2026-10-03): when the game's file arrived in its ROM folder: the file's creation time (unix ms), from disk, so a
-- rebuild keeps it. NULL until a scan reads it. A rescan updates it when it changes (the file deleted and copied again).
ALTER TABLE games ADD COLUMN added_ms INTEGER;

-- 0003 (M4)
ALTER TABLE metadata ADD COLUMN title TEXT;   -- games.title also holds it, for the grid query; a lone disc keeps " (Disc n)"
CREATE TABLE scrape_state (                   -- no row = never scraped
  game_id     INTEGER PRIMARY KEY REFERENCES games(game_id) ON DELETE CASCADE,
  status      TEXT NOT NULL,                  -- 'ok' | 'partial' | 'not_found' | 'error'
  providers   TEXT,                           -- the providers that found it, in order: 'screenscraper,igdb'
  scraped_at  INTEGER NOT NULL
) STRICT;
```

`metadata.source` holds the providers that supplied fields (`'screenscraper,igdb'`), and `scraper_matches` and `scrape_log` take `igdb` too.

**Restoring scraped data (M4).** When a scan adds a game (after a rebuild, a recreated library, or a ROM moved back), its saved responses, if any were saved (`--save-responses`), are read back offline: each provider's parser turns its response into a game, the results merge in config order as a live scrape does, and matches return with their method. Media needs no restoring: the scan indexes the media folder.

```sql
CREATE TABLE systems (
  system_id   TEXT PRIMARY KEY,               -- config id, e.g. 'megadrive'
  scanned_at  INTEGER,                        -- unix ms; NULL = never scanned
  game_count  INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 0      -- denormalised for the boot query
) STRICT;

CREATE TABLE rom_dirs (
  dir_id      INTEGER PRIMARY KEY,
  system_id   TEXT NOT NULL REFERENCES systems(system_id) ON DELETE CASCADE,
  position    INTEGER NOT NULL,               -- order in the system's folder list; earlier folders win path_key collisions
  path        TEXT NOT NULL,                  -- resolved absolute folder at last scan
  UNIQUE (system_id, path)
) STRICT;

CREATE TABLE games (
  game_id     INTEGER PRIMARY KEY,
  system_id   TEXT NOT NULL REFERENCES systems(system_id) ON DELETE CASCADE,
  dir_id      INTEGER NOT NULL REFERENCES rom_dirs(dir_id) ON DELETE CASCADE,
  rel_path    TEXT NOT NULL,                  -- as on disk: relative to its ROM folder, '/' separators, NFC
  path_key    TEXT NOT NULL,                  -- lower-invariant rel_path: the stable identity
  size_bytes  INTEGER NOT NULL,
  mtime_ms    INTEGER NOT NULL,
  crc32 TEXT, md5 TEXT, sha1 TEXT,            -- NULL until hash matching lands; cleared when size or mtime change
  title       TEXT NOT NULL,                  -- scraped title, else cleaned file name (user overrides live in userdata)
  sort_title  TEXT NOT NULL,                  -- internal sort key (TitleParser.SortKey)
  region      TEXT,                           -- file name tags, as written: 'USA, Europe'
  languages   TEXT,                           -- 'En,Fr,De'
  revision    TEXT,                           -- 'Rev 1', 'v1.1'
  disc        INTEGER,                        -- '(Disc 2)' on a disc that no .m3u groups
  tags        TEXT,                           -- every other tag, as written: '(Beta) [b1]'
  UNIQUE (system_id, path_key)
) STRICT;
CREATE INDEX games_by_system ON games(system_id, sort_title);   -- the games grid
CREATE INDEX games_by_dir ON games(dir_id);                      -- ON DELETE CASCADE from rom_dirs

-- Parsed .m3u/.cue/.gdi files, hidden ones included, so an unchanged playlist isn't re-read.
CREATE TABLE playlists (
  system_id   TEXT NOT NULL REFERENCES systems(system_id) ON DELETE CASCADE,
  path_key    TEXT NOT NULL,
  size_bytes  INTEGER NOT NULL,
  mtime_ms    INTEGER NOT NULL,
  refs        TEXT NOT NULL,                  -- referenced path_keys, '\n'-separated
  PRIMARY KEY (system_id, path_key)
) STRICT, WITHOUT ROWID;

CREATE TABLE metadata (
  game_id      INTEGER PRIMARY KEY REFERENCES games(game_id) ON DELETE CASCADE,
  description  TEXT, release_date TEXT,       -- ISO 8601, partial allowed ('1991', '1991-06')
  developer TEXT, publisher TEXT, genre TEXT, players TEXT,
  rating       REAL,                          -- 0..1
  source       TEXT NOT NULL                  -- scraper id
) STRICT;

CREATE TABLE scraper_matches (
  game_id          INTEGER NOT NULL REFERENCES games(game_id) ON DELETE CASCADE,
  scraper          TEXT NOT NULL,             -- 'screenscraper' | 'igdb' | 'steamgriddb' | 'steam'
  scraper_game_id  TEXT NOT NULL,
  method           TEXT NOT NULL,             -- 'filename' | 'hash' | 'manual'
  matched_at       INTEGER NOT NULL,
  PRIMARY KEY (game_id, scraper)
) STRICT;

CREATE TABLE media (                          -- one row per kind: the game's file in the media folder
  game_id  INTEGER NOT NULL REFERENCES games(game_id) ON DELETE CASCADE,
  kind     TEXT NOT NULL,                     -- cover | back | spine | box_texture | label | screenshot | logo | hero | video | model
  path     TEXT NOT NULL,                     -- relative to DataDir: media/<system>/<folder>/...
  width INTEGER, height INTEGER,
  source   TEXT NOT NULL,                     -- dropped by 0004: the media folder doesn't say where a file came from
  PRIMARY KEY (game_id, kind)
) STRICT;

CREATE TABLE scrape_log (
  game_id       INTEGER NOT NULL REFERENCES games(game_id) ON DELETE CASCADE,
  scraper       TEXT NOT NULL,
  status        TEXT NOT NULL,                -- 'ok' | 'not_found' | 'error'
  attempted_at  INTEGER NOT NULL,
  detail        TEXT,
  PRIMARY KEY (game_id, scraper)
) STRICT;
```

From 2026-10-01 `media.kind` can also be `video`: a scraped MP4, with no width or height (NULL), no derivative, and no slot; from 2026-10-02 the game's details screen plays it. From 2026-10-06 it can be `manual` too, a PDF, the same way; from 2026-10-27 the details screen shows it. No schema change.

### `userdata.db` (schema version 5)

This DB can't be rebuilt. It's keyed by `(system_id, path_key)`, never by `library.db` ids. The shipped files are `Data/Migrations/User/0001_initial.sql`, `0002_scraping.sql` (M4), `0003_game_template.sql` (2026-10-07), `0004_astrocade_id.sql` (2026-10-16, a renamed system id) and `0005_sort_name.sql` (2026-11-09):

```sql
-- 0005 (2026-11-09): the sort title the user typed (its metadata panel) or a gamelist import brought. NULL = it sorts
-- by its title. sort_title is the key (TitleParser.SortKey) of sort_name, else of title, so the grid queries are unchanged.
ALTER TABLE game_overrides ADD COLUMN sort_name TEXT;
-- 0003 (2026-10-07): the template the user chose for the game (A7 Resolution order), a template id in the active
-- theme, else the base theme. NULL = its system's. The grid queries return it with each row.
ALTER TABLE game_overrides ADD COLUMN template TEXT;
-- 0002 (M4): the user's metadata overrides, which scraping never writes (NULL = the scraped value)
ALTER TABLE game_overrides ADD COLUMN description TEXT;  -- and release_date, developer, publisher, genre, players (TEXT), rating (REAL)
-- The scrape queue: a batch survives the app closing. Jobs are deleted as they finish; a batch keeps its counts.
CREATE TABLE scrape_batches (batch_id INTEGER PRIMARY KEY, kind TEXT NOT NULL,   -- 'game' | 'system' | 'missing' | 'manual' (2026-10-01)
                             target TEXT, priority INTEGER NOT NULL,             -- single games (0) before batches (1)
                             total INTEGER NOT NULL, done INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 0, failed INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 0,
                             created_at INTEGER NOT NULL, finished_at INTEGER,   -- NULL = resume it
                             cancelled INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 0) STRICT;
CREATE TABLE scrape_jobs    (batch_id INTEGER NOT NULL REFERENCES scrape_batches(batch_id) ON DELETE CASCADE,
                             seq INTEGER NOT NULL, system_id TEXT NOT NULL, path_key TEXT NOT NULL,
                             PRIMARY KEY (batch_id, seq)) STRICT, WITHOUT ROWID;
```

**Clearing a game (M4)** deletes its scraped metadata, scrape state and log, every match (manual ones too), every media row, the title, sort title and metadata overrides, its media files (scraped or the user's own, and from M6 its model, with the model's processed copy in `CacheDir/models/`) and their derivatives, and its saved responses, except a file another game also uses (matched by stem; from 2026-10-06 also a file named without the ROM's extension when another ROM of that name is in the library, indexed or not). The title goes back to the file name. Favourite, play history, emulator override, chosen template (from 2026-10-07) and hidden stay.

```sql
CREATE TABLE favourites     (system_id TEXT NOT NULL, path_key TEXT NOT NULL, added_at INTEGER NOT NULL,
                             PRIMARY KEY (system_id, path_key)) STRICT, WITHOUT ROWID;
CREATE TABLE play_stats     (system_id TEXT NOT NULL, path_key TEXT NOT NULL,
                             play_count INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 0, total_seconds INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 0,
                             last_played_at INTEGER, PRIMARY KEY (system_id, path_key)) STRICT, WITHOUT ROWID;
CREATE INDEX play_stats_recent ON play_stats(last_played_at DESC) WHERE last_played_at IS NOT NULL;   -- Recently played
CREATE TABLE play_sessions  (session_id INTEGER PRIMARY KEY, system_id TEXT NOT NULL, path_key TEXT NOT NULL,
                             emulator TEXT NOT NULL, started_at INTEGER NOT NULL,
                             ended_at INTEGER, exit_code INTEGER) STRICT;      -- ended_at NULL = launcher died; closed on next start
CREATE INDEX play_sessions_open ON play_sessions(session_id) WHERE ended_at IS NULL;
CREATE TABLE manual_matches (system_id TEXT NOT NULL, path_key TEXT NOT NULL, scraper TEXT NOT NULL,
                             scraper_game_id TEXT NOT NULL, matched_at INTEGER NOT NULL,   -- the latest is asked first (2026-10-01)
                             PRIMARY KEY (system_id, path_key, scraper)) STRICT, WITHOUT ROWID;
CREATE TABLE game_overrides (system_id TEXT NOT NULL, path_key TEXT NOT NULL,
                             title TEXT, sort_title TEXT,                     -- NULL = use the scraped or file-name title; sort_title: the key
                             emulator TEXT,                                   -- NULL = the system default
                             hidden INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 0,
                             PRIMARY KEY (system_id, path_key)) STRICT, WITHOUT ROWID;
```

**Play history (M3).**
- A session row is written when the emulator has started, with `ended_at` NULL.
- When the game ends, one transaction closes it (`ended_at` = start + the duration measured on a monotonic clock, `exit_code`) and adds to `play_stats`: `play_count` + 1, `total_seconds` + the duration rounded to the nearest second, and `last_played_at` = the session's end.
- A failed launch (a non-zero exit within 5 s) keeps its session row with its exit code, but doesn't change `play_stats`.
- Opening the library closes sessions a crashed launcher left open, at their start time. Each counts as a play with no play time, because how long the game ran isn't known.

### Grid queries

Each selects only what a cell draws: game id, effective title, cover path and root, and the favourite flag.
- **A system:** `games` through `games_by_system`, LEFT JOINed on primary keys to `user.game_overrides`, `user.favourites` and `media` (kind `cover`), hidden games left out, ordered by `COALESCE(o.sort_title, g.sort_title)`. An overridden title, or a sort title (2026-11-09: `o.sort_title` is the key of the user's sort title, else of their title), sorts in its own place, at the cost of a temporary B-tree sort (2.4 ms for 10,000 rows). Each row carries the sort title too, which the grid's letter jump goes by, so a game's letter group is the one it sorts in.
- **A system in another order (2026-10-03, `[display] games_sort` or the system's own, A5):** the same query, joining what it sorts by (`user.play_stats` for last played and time played; `metadata` and the user's `release_date` override, which wins, for the release date; `games.added_ms` for added), sorted in a temporary B-tree: 20 to 27 ms for 10,000 games against 21 for title order (core-bench, export runtime). Descending reverses what the sort names; ties go by title, A to Z, then by id; a game never played (by last played), without a release date (ISO 8601 text, so `'1991'` sorts before `'1991-06'`), or whose creation time was never read comes last either way; a game never played has no play time (0).
- **Favourites (2026-11-10):** All games' query in Favourites' own order (`[display.favourites]`, else `[display] games_sort`), with `f.added_at IS NOT NULL` added to its condition, which makes the LEFT JOIN on `user.favourites` an inner one. It was `user.favourites` joined to `games` in title order.
- **Recently played:** `user.play_stats` through `play_stats_recent`, newest first, with a limit.
- **All games (2026-10-15):** a system's query in All games' own order (`[display.all_games]`, 2026-11-10, else `[display] games_sort`) without the system condition, each row with its `system_id`, sorted in a temporary B-tree whatever the order; rows of systems config no longer has are left out, as in the two above. Its media are one query on `media` by kind, not by game id.
- **A group's card (2026-11-03, A5 Grouped systems):** All games' query with `g.system_id IN ($s0, ...)` for the group's systems, each row with its `system_id`, in the group's system's order; its media are a system's media query with the same condition.

### Migrations (versioned)

- Each DB has its own ordered SQL files, embedded in Core (`Data/Migrations/{Library,User}/NNNN_name.sql`). A shipped file is never edited.
- The runner follows SQLite's documented procedure:
  1. `foreign_keys=OFF` before `BEGIN`.
  2. Apply every migration newer than `user_version`.
  3. `foreign_key_check`.
  4. Bump `user_version` and commit.
- `userdata.db` is copied with `VACUUM INTO` first (`userdata.db.v<from>-<utc time>.bak`, next to it), and the last 3 backups are kept.
- If a DB is newer than the app, or a migration fails:
  - `library.db` is rebuilt.
  - `userdata.db` is refused with a clear error.

## A5. Config files (TOML)

Our own keys use UK spelling (`colour`, `favourite`).

### `settings.toml`

```toml
format = 1

[paths]
rom_root = "D:/ROMs"                 # default "{home}/ROMs"
media = "E:/Launcher media"          # 2026-10-04: the media folder (A4). Unset (default): DataDir/media. Settings > Library > Media folder moves it

[scanning]
# Applied to every system, before its own `exclude`. Default: ES-DE's media folders and gamelists.
# Setting a list replaces the default; [] turns it off.
exclude = ["images", "manuals", "videos", "gamelist.xml"]
scan_at_launch = false               # 2026-10-08: true rescans every system after interactive, at each start; false (default) only systems never scanned. Settings > Library. Either is "Rescan the library" (2026-10-18: media folders only for systems with new games)

[variables]                          # expanded at config load; may use {home} and each other (cycles are errors)
retroarch = "C:/Emulators/RetroArch" # built-in defaults: retroarch = "C:/RetroArch-Win64", emulators = "C:/Emulators"
# tools = ["{home}/a", "/opt/b"]     # 2026-11-03: a list of places, for an emulator's executable and core only (Finding programs); Linux's built-in emulators, flatpak, retroarch and retroarch_cores are lists

[display]
theme = "slab"                       # default "slab" (2026-10-08; "console" before). "memory-card" warns and uses the default
screen_mode = "borderless"           # 2026-10-08: "borderless" (default; Godot's Fullscreen), "fullscreen" (exclusive) or "windowed". Replaces fullscreen = true/false, still read when screen_mode is missing (false is "windowed")
render_resolution = "auto"           # the 3D's height in the fullscreen modes: "auto" (default, at most 1080p: A3), "native" or 240–4320; never above the window's. A window uses "auto"
window_size = "1280x800"             # the window's size when windowed, 640x360 to 7680x7680; clamped to the screen's usable area
rendering_driver = "d3d12"           # "d3d12" (default) or "vulkan"; from the next start, through override.cfg beside the executable (A3 Rendering); Windows only: Linux has Vulkan alone
anisotropic_filtering = 16           # 0 (off), 2, 4, 8 or 16 (default; the most D3D12 and Vulkan allow)
hide_empty_systems = true            # the systems grid leaves out systems with no games (default); false shows every enabled system. Settings > Appearance > Show systems without games (2026-10-27; Yes is false)
group_systems = true                 # 2026-11-03: a system with a group (a regional variant) shows its games in its group's card (default); false gives every system its own (A5 Grouped systems). Settings > Appearance > Group regional variants
systems_layout = "grid"              # 2026-10-02: "grid" (default), "grid_with_labels" (2026-11-02: the grid, each system's name under it), "carousel" (one row, the focused one in the middle), "single" (one at a time) or "coverflow" (2026-11-06: one row, the focused one facing out, the others side on)
systems_columns = 0                  # the grid's columns (with labels or without), 1-9; 0 (default) automatic: as many as fit
systems_rows = 0                     # the grid's rows that fit between the heading and the details, 1-6; 0 (default) automatic
games_layout = "grid"                # "grid" (default), "grid_with_labels" (each game's title under it), "carousel", "list" (the titles, the focused game's model beside them), "coverflow" (2026-11-06: one row of spines, the focused game turned to face out) or "showcase" (2026-11-06: a strip of games, the focused game's art and details above it)
games_columns = 0                    # as above; a system's own games_layout, games_columns and games_rows (systems.toml) win
games_rows = 0
systems_sort = "alphabetical"        # 2026-10-03: "alphabetical" (default), "manufacturer", "release_year" or "manufacturer_year"
systems_sort_order = "ascending"     # or "descending"; Favourites, Recently played and All games always come first
games_sort = "alphabetical"          # "alphabetical" (default), "last_played", "play_time", "added" or "release_date"
games_sort_order = "ascending"       # or "descending"; a system's own games_sort and games_sort_order (systems.toml) win

[display.favourites]                 # 2026-11-10: user only; Favourites' own view and order, with a system's keys: games_layout, games_columns, games_rows, games_sort, games_sort_order. Unset: [display]'s. X on its card, or Menu in its games
[display.all_games]                  # 2026-11-10: the same for All games. Recently played has none: newest first, in [display] games_layout

[ui]                                 # the status indicators, top right (2026-10-02), the navigation sounds, the button icons and the UI's size
show_clock = true                    # the time, in the user's regional short-time format
show_battery = true                  # the battery's charge; nothing shows on a device without one
show_network = true                  # Wi-Fi with its signal, a cable, or disconnected
# show_performance                   # 2026-10-08 to 2026-11-07: the FPS and VRAM readout, removed; a file that has it loads without a warning, and it does nothing
navigation_sounds = true             # 2026-10-10: a tock when the focus moves, a whoosh when a system is entered (A1 Ui, UiSounds); defaults to true
button_icons = "automatic"           # 2026-11-05: the controls' button icons (A1 Button icons): the device last used's, or always xbox, playstation, nintendo, gamecube, steamdeck, steamcontroller or keyboard
size = "medium"                      # 2026-11-05: the 2D UI's size (A1 UI size): small (0.85), medium or large (1.2); the 3D grids keep theirs

[scraping]
provider = "screenscraper"           # asked first for every game
fallback = ["launchbox", "igdb", "steamgriddb"]   # then these, in order, for what's still missing; "launchbox" from 2026-11-01; "steam" (the Steam store) is opt-in
regions = ["eu", "wor", "us", "jp"]  # ScreenScraper region codes; first available wins (IGDB's release regions and LaunchBox's regions map from them); Settings > Scraping > Preferred regions (2026-10-04)
languages = ["en"]
media = ["cover", "back", "spine", "screenshot", "logo"]   # the default; also hero (fan art), label (a disc's or cartridge's art), video, manual
hash_limit_mb = 64                   # ROMs up to this size are hashed for ScreenScraper; 0 = none
```

**Sorting (2026-10-03).**
- **Systems** (`systems_sort`): by name, by manufacturer, by the year the system came out, or by manufacturer then year (the systems' `manufacturer` and `year`). Descending reverses what the sort names (the manufacturer and the year, or the name for alphabetical); ties are always broken by name, A to Z; names and manufacturers compare ignoring case, numbers in order ("PlayStation 2" before "PlayStation 10"); a system without the manufacturer or year the sort needs comes last either way (`Library/SystemOrder`). Favourites, Recently played and All games aren't systems and always come first.
- **Games** (`games_sort`, and a system's own): by title, when last played, the time spent playing (`play_stats`), when added (the file's creation time: when it arrived in the ROM folder, kept by the scan) or the release date (scraped, or the user's own). Ties go by title; what's missing comes last (A4 Grid queries). Favourites and All games follow `games_sort` too, unless they have their own (`[display.favourites]`, `[display.all_games]`, 2026-11-10; a bad value there is a warning, as a system's is); Recently played is always newest first.
- A bad value in `[display]` is an error and the default is used, like a layout; one of the other screen's sorts says so (`'play_time' sorts games, not systems`). A system's bad `games_sort` or `games_sort_order` is a warning and `[display]`'s is used.

### System definition

Built-in definitions ship in `Launcher.Core/Defaults/systems.toml`: first the original fourteen (Game Boy, Game Boy Color, Game Boy Advance, NES, SNES, N64, GameCube, Master System, Mega Drive, Saturn, Dreamcast, PlayStation, PlayStation 2 and PSP), then 150 more converted from ES-DE (see [The ES-DE catalogue](#the-es-de-catalogue)), and Windows, Steam and Desktop Apps (see [Windows and Steam games](#windows-and-steam-games)), with the PlayStation 5 (2026-10-15) and the PlayStation Vita (2026-10-28; see [Vita3K's games](#vita3ks-games)) added by hand since. The user's `ConfigDir/systems.toml` holds only what changes.

```toml
# built-in
[systems.megadrive]
name = "Mega Drive"
manufacturer = "Sega"
year = 1988
description = "The Mega Drive is a 16-bit fourth-generation home video game console..."   # 2026-10-03
aliases = ["genesis", "md"]
extensions = [".md", ".gen", ".smd", ".bin", ".zip", ".7z"]   # matched ignoring case
emulator = "retroarch-genesis-plus-gx"
alt_emulators = []
game_model = "clamshell"             # M6: user only; a game template id in the theme (else the built-in theme). Unset by default: the theme decides
# games_layout = "list"              # 2026-10-02: user only; how its games are shown. Unset: [display] games_layout
# games_columns = 4                  # 2026-10-02: user only; its games grid's columns and rows (0 automatic). Unset: [display]'s
# games_rows = 3
# games_sort = "release_date"        # 2026-10-03: user only; what its games are sorted by. Unset: [display] games_sort
# games_sort_order = "descending"    # 2026-10-03: user only; which way. Unset: [display] games_sort_order
screenscraper_id = 1
igdb_platforms = [29]                # IGDB platform ids; NES and SNES add their Japanese twins (99, 58)
launchbox_platforms = ["Sega Genesis"]   # 2026-11-01: LaunchBox platform names (A5 LaunchBox Games DB)
# steam_store = true                 # only on windows and steam: looked up on the Steam store
# auto_configure_controllers = true  # 2026-10-10: on for switch; the pads are set up in the emulator before each launch
# swap_ab_xy = true                  # 2026-10-11: with it, an Xbox-style pad's A (at the bottom) is the Switch's A
# group = "dreamcast"                # 2026-11-03: built in on the regional variants only (dreamcastnajp; segacd and megacdjp, "megacd"; sfc, "snes"); its games show in that system's card (A5 Grouped systems). "" takes it out

# user override
[systems.megadrive]
rom_dirs = ["E:/Sega/Mega Drive", "{rom_root}/genesis"]   # default: the first of {rom_root}/megadrive, /genesis, /md that exists
recursive = true                     # default
exclude = ["bios", "**/Unused/*"]    # added to [scanning] exclude; globs against paths relative to a ROM folder; no '/' = any file or folder name
emulator = "retroarch-mesen"

[systems.mastersystem]
enabled = false
```

- `screenscraper_id` values were checked on 2026-09-28 against ScreenScraper's own system pages (`systemeinfos.php?plateforme=<id>`) and ES-DE's table, and every built-in one on 2026-10-03 against `systemesListe.php` (which needs developer credentials) with `odyssey-scrape ss-systems`: it lists each system's id beside ScreenScraper's names for it, then ScreenScraper's systems that no system uses.
- `igdb_platforms` (M4) were cross-checked between RomM's IGDB platform table and an IGDB platform list. A system without them isn't looked up on IGDB.
- `launchbox_platforms` (2026-11-01) are LaunchBox's platform names, as its `Platforms.xml` writes them ("Sega Genesis", "Sony Playstation 2"); a system's games are looked up only among them. 145 of the 169 built-in systems have them (the arcade boards all "Arcade"; MSX, Neo Geo and the PSP list each of LaunchBox's machines); the 24 LaunchBox has no platform for (the game engines, the fantasy consoles but PICO-8 and WASM-4, the phones, the Thomson computers, MSX turbo R, the ZX Spectrum Next, Gamate, Console Arcade, PC Arcade, MESS, LCD games and Ports) have none and aren't looked up. A user's list replaces the built-in one. `LaunchBoxTests` checks every built-in name against LaunchBox's platform list (`Scraping/Fixtures/launchbox_platforms.txt`).
- `games_layout`, `games_columns` and `games_rows` (2026-10-02; unset by default) give a system's games their own layout (`"grid"`, `"grid_with_labels"`, `"carousel"`, `"list"`, `"coverflow"` or `"showcase"`) and grid size (used when its layout is a grid, with labels or without). A bad value (an unknown layout, not an integer, or outside 0–9 and 0–6) is a warning and `[display]`'s is used: a matter of taste mustn't disable the system, as an error would.
- `games_sort` and `games_sort_order` (2026-10-03; unset by default) give a system's games their own order (A5 Sorting), each on its own; a bad value is a warning and `[display]`'s is used.
- `manufacturer` and `year` (the year the system first came out anywhere) are shown under the focused system's name, and on its details screen (Y), and sort the systems grid (`systems_sort`).
- `description` (2026-10-03) is a paragraph about the system, shown whole on its details screen (Y; until that screen, the overlay showed four lines of it). A user's replaces the built-in one; an empty one is none.
- `steam_store` (default false; true on `windows` and `steam`) lets the Steam store provider look the system's games up by title. It's off elsewhere so a console game can't match its PC re-release.
- `auto_configure_controllers` (2026-10-10; default false, true on `switch`) sets the connected pads up in the emulator's own config before each launch, when the emulator that launches the game has `controllers` in its profile (below); with another emulator it does nothing. The system's options (X) turn it on and off.
- `swap_ab_xy` (2026-10-11; default false) switches A/B and X/Y in that setup on pads not labelled as a Switch's, so the button labelled A on an Xbox pad is the Switch's A (A5 Eden's controllers). The system's options call it "Switch mapping for A/B and X/Y".
- `group` (2026-11-03; built in only on the regional variants) names the system whose card this system's games show in while `[display] group_systems` is on. See [Grouped systems](#grouped-systems-2026-11-03).
- Every config file may start with `format = 1`.

### Emulator profiles

Built-in profiles ship in `Defaults/emulators.windows.toml` and `Defaults/emulators.linux.toml`, one complete file for each OS, its emulators being other programs in other places (2026-11-03; [Built-in emulators on Linux](#built-in-emulators-on-linux-2026-11-03)), using variables like `{retroarch}`. A profile with the same emulator has the same id and name in both, so `systems.toml` names it on either. The user's `ConfigDir/emulators.toml` (one file, whatever the OS) layers over the OS's.

```toml
[emulators.retroarch-genesis-plus-gx]
name = "RetroArch: Genesis Plus GX"
executable = "{retroarch}/retroarch.exe"
core = "{retroarch}/cores/genesis_plus_gx_libretro.dll"   # a path, like executable; {core} expands to it
args = ["-L", "{core}", "--fullscreen", "{rom}"]

[emulators.blastem]
name = "BlastEm"
executable = "C:/Emulators/BlastEm/blastem.exe"
args = ["-f", "--rom={rom}"]         # placeholders can sit anywhere inside an entry
working_dir = "{emulator_dir}"       # default

[emulators.run-file]                 # a game that is its own program, script or shortcut
name = "Run the game's own file (shortcut, script or program)"
run_file = true                      # no executable, core or args; working_dir defaults to "{rom_dir}"
```

- A game launches with, in order: an emulator chosen for that launch, the game's own override (`game_overrides.emulator` in `userdata.db`), or the system's `emulator`. The settings screen offers a system only its built-in `emulator` and its `alt_emulators` (2026-10-07; another profile can still be named in systems.toml), and a game's options offer the same, after "the system's emulator" (2026-10-18), with the game's own override if it's none of them; any configured profile still launches as an override set before. An override naming a profile that's gone is a launch error that says where it was named.
- `controllers = "eden"` (2026-10-10; only on the built-in `eden`) says how the launcher sets the profile's controllers up for `auto_configure_controllers`. `"eden"` is the only kind; another is a warning and ignored, as it is on a `run_file` profile. See [Eden's controllers](#edens-controllers).
- `installs = "vita3k"` and `install_args` (2026-10-28; only on the built-in `vita3k`): the emulator runs games installed in its own storage, so the launcher boots a game installed there with `args` and launches one that isn't with `install_args`, for the emulator to install and then run. `"vita3k"` is the only kind (it says where Vita3K keeps them); another is a warning and ignored, as it is on a `run_file` profile, and every game is then launched with `args`. `install_args` is checked as `args` is, a `run_file` profile can't have it, and without `installs` it's a warning and ignored; a profile with `installs` but no `install_args` can't launch a game that isn't installed. See [Vita3K's games](#vita3ks-games).
- The built-in RetroArch core file names and `pcsx2-qt.exe` were checked against a RetroBat install (2026-09-28). The flags follow each emulator's documentation; a launch with each real emulator is still to do (M3 log).

### Finding programs (2026-11-03)

On Linux an emulator isn't in one folder: it's a distribution's package on the PATH, a Flatpak, or an AppImage with its version in its name. So a profile's `executable` and `core` are each a place to look or a list of them, and the first place that has the file wins (`Launching/ProgramSearch`):

```toml
[emulators.duckstation]                 # Linux's built-in profile
executable = ["duckstation-nogui", "duckstation-qt", "{emulators}/DuckStation*.AppImage", "{flatpak}/org.duckstation.DuckStation"]
```

- **A place** is a bare name (no folder), looked for in the PATH's folders in order (relative PATH entries are skipped: they'd depend on the launcher's working folder); a path whose file name has a `*`, matching the files in its folder, the most recently written winning (the newest is the one just installed when two versions sit side by side; a folder with a `*` matches nothing); or a path that must exist. A bare name stays as it is; anything else is made absolute as before. Until 2026-11-03 a bare name was a path under ConfigDir.
- **A list variable** (`[variables] tools = ["{home}/a", "/opt/b"]`) makes an entry using it one place for each of its items (`"{tools}/emu"`). Only an emulator's `executable` and `core` can use one, and an entry can use one; anywhere else (args, `working_dir`, `rom_dirs`, another variable) it's an error. Its items can use `{home}` and string variables. Linux's built-in settings (`Defaults/settings.linux.toml`) make `emulators` (`~/Applications`, `~/.local/share/applications`, `~/.local/bin`, `~/bin`), `flatpak` (the system's and the user's exports), `retroarch` (RetroArch's places, written out: a list can't use another) and `retroarch_cores` (where RetroArch's builds and packages keep their cores) lists. A user's string replaces a list as any value does.
- **When:** at launch (`LaunchService`, before the plan, which then has the found program and core, so `{emulator_dir}` is the found one's folder), on the Emulators page (each row's program where it was found) and in the install check (each place checked once). Nothing is found at boot. A program in none of its places is a launch error naming them, as a core is; a profile with one place is checked with the plan, as before.
- **The settings:** with `{retroarch}` a list there are no folder rows; choosing a program for every profile sharing a list (`EmulatorConfig.ProgramVariable`) sets that variable to it, so the cores stay where `{retroarch_cores}` finds them.
- **A working folder that isn't there yet is made** (2026-11-03): Linux's MAME starts in `~/.mame`. One that can't be made is the launch error it was.
- **A game's own file on Linux** (`run_file`, `PortableProcessRunner`): a `.desktop` shortcut runs its `[Desktop Entry]` `Exec` line, split as the Desktop Entry Specification says, field codes left out (`Launching/DesktopEntry`), in its `Path` folder if it names one; a `.sh` script runs with bash (else sh), so it needn't be executable; anything else (an AppImage) runs as it is. A Steam shortcut (`Exec=steam steam://rungameid/<id>`) starts its game, but Steam's own process is followed, which ends at once when Steam is running (L3: Steam on Linux).

### Eden's controllers

Eden maps no pad until the user picks it in its settings, so a pad that isn't set up does nothing in a game. With `auto_configure_controllers`, before each launch the launcher writes Eden's `[Controls]` as Eden's own settings would after picking each pad (RetroBat's emulatorLauncher does the same, from EmulationStation's input config):

- **Where.** `user/config/qt-config.ini` beside `eden.exe` when that `user` folder exists (Eden's portable mode), else `%APPDATA%\eden\config\qt-config.ini` (Eden's `path_util.cpp`; on Linux, the working folder's `user`, else `$XDG_CONFIG_HOME/eden`). A missing file is created. The first time it's changed, a copy is kept beside it (`qt-config.ini.odyssey-backup`), and it's written to a temporary file and moved over the old one. Every other line stays as it was (Eden reads it with SimpleIni: keys ignore case and the first of a repeated key wins, so a key is replaced where it is and its repeats removed).
- **Which pads, as whom.** The pads Godot sees, in device order, the one that chose the game first: player 1 is the pad in your hand. Up to 8 players, each a Pro Controller (`type` 0) and connected; players after the last pad are disconnected (their bindings are kept), and the handheld slot (`player_8`) is left alone. Left out, with the reason logged: a pad whose mapping isn't known (below); a Joy-Con, which Eden reads with its own driver; a Pro Controller when Eden's `enable_procon_driver` is on; and, when a Steam Deck's own controls are connected, an Xbox 360 pad (`045e:028e`), taken to be the virtual pad Steam Input or a handheld tool makes of them, which would get every press a second time. If no pad is left, the file isn't touched.
- **Names.** Godot reads pads through SDL3 (3.2 in Godot 4.7) as Eden does (3.4), and both leave SDL's Windows defaults for which driver opens a pad (XInput, HIDAPI, DirectInput; RawInput, Windows.Gaming.Input and GameInput off), so they see a pad under the same GUID. Eden names it by that GUID with the name's CRC (bytes 2-3) cleared, and its `port`: which of the pads with that GUID it is, in the order SDL opened them (Godot's device order). Eden's `enable_raw_input`, which would rename XInput pads, is turned off if it's on.
- **Bindings.** Eden binds raw joystick inputs: for each Switch button, the input SDL's mapping gives the matching gamepad element (A the east face button, B south, X north, Y west, where a Switch has them; with `swap_ab_xy`, A south, B east, X west and Y north on a pad whose labels aren't a Switch's, below; ZL and ZR the triggers; SL and SR the shoulders; Home the guide button; Capture `misc1`), each written as Eden writes them (`engine:sdl,port:0,guid:...,button:1`, `...,hat:0,direction:up`, `...,axis:2,threshold:0.5,invert:+`), the sticks with no centre offset (Eden's default deadzone and range), and motion from the pad for the HIDAPI pads with a gyro (PlayStation, Switch Pro, the Steam Deck, the Steam Controller). The launcher carries SDL 3.4.8's mappings for the pads SDL maps without its database on Windows: its XInput mapping (every XInput pad), and the one it generates for a HIDAPI pad (a standard layout, with the variants for 8BitDo's swapped face buttons, GameCube-style pads and adapters, and Nintendo's NES, SNES, N64, Mega Drive and Wii controllers). A DirectInput pad's mapping is in SDL's controller database, which the launcher doesn't carry, so it's left out; map it once in Eden.
- **Labels** (2026-10-11). SDL's face buttons are positions, so by default each pad plays as a Switch would by position: an Xbox pad's B (on the right) is the Switch's A. `swap_ab_xy` switches A/B and X/Y on the pads whose labels aren't a Switch's: XInput, PlayStation, the Steam Deck, the generic HIDAPI layout, and GameCube pads (whose A is in the middle, SDL's south). Pads labelled as a Switch's are left as they are, since their labels already match: Nintendo's own HIDAPI pads but its GameCube ones (and a Bluetooth Switch Pro without ids), and 8BitDo's Nintendo-style pads. RetroBat's `switch_gamepadbuttons` does the same, inverted for Nintendo's pads because EmulationStation's input config is by label. A licensed Switch pad from another maker (HORI, PowerA) is taken to be Xbox-style.

### Vita3K's games

The PlayStation Vita (`psvita`, 2026-10-28) is run by Vita3K, which runs games installed in its own storage, its emulated memory card: `-r <title id>` boots one installed there, and a package (`.vpk`, `.zip`) or a game's folder given on its command line is installed, then booted. It installs whatever it's given again every time, over the copy it has (its command line passes no "already installed" choice), which for a Vita game is a copy of up to a few gigabytes. So the built-in profile has both, and the launcher picks:

```toml
[emulators.vita3k]
name = "Vita3K"
executable = "{emulators}/Vita3K/Vita3K.exe"
args = ["--frontend=sdl", "-F", "-r", "{title_id}"]   # a game installed in Vita3K
install_args = ["--frontend=sdl", "-F", "{rom}"]      # one that isn't: Vita3K installs it, then boots it
installs = "vita3k"                                   # how the launcher knows which
```

- **The title id** (`{title_id}`, `Launching/TitleId`): nine letters and digits, as Sony numbers its games and as Vita3K names an installed game's folder. Read, in order, from the game's name, the last such tag in square brackets, as dumps carry it and RetroBat reads it (`Gravity Rush (Europe) [PCSF00024].vpk`); from a file of at most 4,096 bytes, its first word that is one (ES-DE's `.psvita` file holds the id, and ES-DE reads at most 4,096 bytes of a file it injects); from a game's folder's `sce_sys/param.sfo`; or from the `sce_sys/param.sfo` nearest the root of a package, read from the zip's directory and that one entry (the game's own, not an update's it may also hold). For a folder game (`Game.psvita/`), it's read from the folder's file of its own name when it has one (ES-DE's layout), else the folder (`FolderGame`). The owner's 76 packages, named in the first way, give the same id from their `param.sfo`, in about 20 ms each over the network (5.3 GB, Zip64, the largest). A profile that uses `{title_id}` for a game with none fails before anything starts, saying where to put it.
- **Installed or not** (`Launching/Vita3KGames`): installed when `ux0/app/<title id>` is a folder in Vita3K's storage, which is found as Vita3K's start-up finds it (its `app_init.cpp` and `config.cpp`, read on 2026-10-28): `portable/fs` when a `portable` folder is beside `Vita3K.exe` (portable mode, whatever its config says); else the `pref-path` in `config.yml` beside it, when set (a relative one from the executable's folder; RetroBat sets it); else SDL's preference folder, `%APPDATA%\Vita3K\Vita3K`. A game with no title id counts as not installed. **On Linux** (L3b, 2026-11-04; its `app_init.cpp` and `config.cpp` read again on 2026-11-04, master `f34f11e`) Vita3K's rules differ, and `LinuxStorageFolder` follows them: `portable/fs` when a `portable` folder is beside the program, its links followed (an AppImage's is beside the `.AppImage` file, which Vita3K reads from `$APPIMAGE`; any other build's beside its real program, SDL's base folder being `/proc/self/exe`'s); else the `pref-path` in `config.yml` in its XDG config folder, `$XDG_CONFIG_HOME/Vita3K` (`~/.config/Vita3K`), not beside the program; else its XDG data folder's `Vita3K`, `$XDG_DATA_HOME/Vita3K/Vita3K` (`~/.local/share/Vita3K/Vita3K`). The XDG folders are .NET's `ApplicationData` and `LocalApplicationData` there, from the environment Vita3K inherits from the launcher. A relative `pref-path` starts from the executable's folder on both OSes, the launch's working folder by default. Flathub has no Vita3K (the owner's decision: no Flatpak rule; one such as RetroDECK's, inside another app's Flatpak, has that app's folders).
- **Not installed:** a `.vpk` or `.zip` package or a game's folder is launched with `install_args`; its next launch finds it installed. Anything else (ES-DE's `.psvita` file, which only names a game) fails before anything starts (`LaunchFailure.MissingFile`), saying the game isn't installed, or that no title id was found, so Vita3K can't be told which game to run.
- **`--frontend=sdl`** (Vita3K from 2026-10-09) runs the game in a plain window that closes with it and quits Vita3K, so the launcher sees the game end; the full interface would stay open after it. Older builds ignore an option they don't know (Vita3K's command line allows extras), and the one-token form keeps them from reading `sdl` as a package to install. `-F` asks for full screen. While a package installs, nothing shows but the launcher's "Running" screen.
- **The system:** extensions `.psvita` (ES-DE's file, or a folder game: ES-DE's `Game.psvita/Game.psvita`, or RetroBat's game folder), `.vpk` and `.zip`; ScreenScraper's 62 (ES-DE's and RomM's tables; not checked with `ss-systems`, which needs the developer credentials) and IGDB's 46 (RomM's IGDB table); year and description from ES-DE's metadata, as the catalogue's.

### The ES-DE catalogue

`Defaults/systems.toml` and `Defaults/emulators.toml` carry every system of ES-DE 3.5.0's Windows `es_systems.xml` (ids, names, extensions, commands) and every emulator of its `es_find_rules.xml`, converted by a script (kept out of the repo; the files are the source of truth from here on). Rules of the conversion:

- **Ids are ES-DE's**, so existing ES-DE folders work. Regional twins and duplicates are aliases of one system, whose folder names are default ROM folders too: `genesis`, `megadrivejp` (Mega Drive), `snesna` (SNES), `famicom` (NES), `mark3` (Master System), `sega32xjp`, `sega32xna` (`sega32x`), `saturnjp`, `neogeocdjp`, `tg16` (`pcengine`), `tg-cd` (`pcenginecd`), `videopac` (`odyssey2`), `fba` (`fbneo`), `mame-advmame` (`mame`), `amiga600`, `amiga1200` (`amiga`), `laserdisc` (`daphne`). Their commands are merged in. (`megacd` and `megacdjp` were `segacd`'s aliases until 2026-11-03, when the three became the Mega-CD and its regional variants, and `sfc` and `superfamicom` `snes`'s, when `sfc` became the Super Famicom, `superfamicom` its alias: [Regional variants](#regional-variants-2026-11-02).) Left out: `emulators`, `kodi`, `epic` (not systems), `windows`, `steam` and `desktop` (added by hand since: below), the placeholder-only `androidapps`, `androidgames`, `lutris`, `xboxone`, and `psvita` (Vita3K needs a title id ES-DE reads from a file; it came back on 2026-10-28 with `{title_id}`: [Vita3K's games](#vita3ks-games)).
- **The first ES-DE command is the default emulator and the others are alternatives**, except that a system whose first command is a shortcut or script (`run-file`) and which has other kinds of files gets its first real emulator as the default (`ports` keeps `run-file`). The original fourteen keep their defaults and gain the others as alternatives.
- **Emulators sit under `{emulators}` with ES-DE's folder names** (`{emulators}/Mesen/Mesen.exe`, from the first static path of its find rule); RetroArch cores under `{retroarch}/cores`. Profile ids: `retroarch-<core>` (`mednafen_*` is `beetle-*`), `<emulator>` when it has one set of arguments or a general one for six or more systems, else `<emulator>-<system>` or `<emulator>-<what its label adds>`. The existing `dolphin`, `pcsx2` and `ppsspp` are reused for those emulators (their flags are the project's), and RetroArch profiles pass `--fullscreen` like the others.
- **Tokens:** `%ROM%`, `%ROMRAW%` `{rom}`; `%GAMEDIR%` `{rom_dir}`; `%BASENAME%` `{rom_stem}`; `%FILENAME%` `{rom_file}`; `%ROMPATH%` `{rom_root}`; `%EMUDIR%` `{emulator_dir}`; `%STARTDIR%=` `working_dir`; `%HIDEWINDOW%`, `%ESCAPESPECIALS%` and `%RUNINBACKGROUND%` dropped; `%EMULATOR_OS-SHELL% /C` becomes the `run-file` profile. `%INJECT%` (extra arguments ES-DE reads from a file beside the ROM) isn't supported: it's dropped where the command works without it, and the command is left out where it supplies required arguments (PS3 and PS4 game serials, Vita3K, 3dSen, Linux Loader, an EKA2L1 custom device). Vita3K's, which injects only the title id, came back as `{title_id}` (2026-10-28). `pcsx2x6` assumes `pcsx2-qt.exe` (ES-DE matches `pcsx2x6*.exe`).
- **The missing manufacturers (2026-10-03)** were added by hand, from memory, unverified (`es_systems.xml` has none).
- **Years and descriptions (2026-10-03) come from ES-DE's theme metadata**, `themes/linear-es-de/system/metadata/<id>.xml` in ES-DE's repository (`systemReleaseYear`, `systemDescription`), converted by a script kept out of the repo. A system's `year` is the earliest of its own file's and its aliases' (Mega Drive 1988 from `megadrivejp`, NES 1983 from `famicom`, SNES 1990 from `sfc`), so it stays the year the system first came out anywhere; its `description` is its own file's English one (`astrocade`'s is `astrocade.xml`), with the paragraphs joined into one, the dates day first and British spelling (`colour`, `-ise`; product names such as Game Boy Color keep theirs). Engines and stores now have years too (MAME 1997, Steam 2003, Windows 1985); only the catch-alls ES-DE dates "Various" have none and come last: `arcade`, `consolearcade`, `desktop`, `lcdgames`, `pcarcade` and `ports`. `ConfigLoaderTests` checks which systems have no year and that every system has a description. Some of ES-DE's years date something other than the hardware: `daphne` is 2007 (the Daphne emulator, where its LaserDisc games began in 1983), and `ti99` is 1981 where its own description says late 1979.
- **`igdb_platforms` of the catalogue systems were written from memory and are not verified**; a system without them isn't looked up on IGDB. **`screenscraper_id` was checked against ScreenScraper's system list (2026-10-03)**: every system ScreenScraper has is given its id, several sharing one where ScreenScraper has a single system for them (`atarixe` is `atari800`'s 43; `pc`, `doom` and `quake` are DOS's 135; `lcdgames` is Game & Watch's 52; `moto` and `to8` are Thomson MO/TO's 141; `tanodragon` is Dragon 32/64's 91; `multivision` is SG-1000's 109; the mixed `cps` is arcade's 75, `cps1`–`cps3` their own 6–8). Twelve have none, because ScreenScraper has no such system: `ags`, `chailove`, `consolearcade`, `flash`, `mess`, `mugen`, `pcarcade`, `ports`, `symbian`, `triforce`, `trs-80` and `zxnext`; they aren't looked up on ScreenScraper.
- The built-in theme gives each a colour (by manufacturer) and the nearest of its boxes (A6; DOS has the big box, shaped by each game's art). A system with no ROMs has no card (`hide_empty_systems`; Settings > Appearance > Show systems without games gives every system one), the Emulators page lists only the profiles that systems with games use (every system's while they're all shown), and install warnings cover only those systems.

### Built-in emulators on Linux (2026-11-03)

Linux has its own built-in emulators file, `Defaults/emulators.linux.toml`, and its own values for the shared systems and settings, `Defaults/systems.linux.toml` and `Defaults/settings.linux.toml`, merged over `systems.toml` and `settings.toml` on Linux only (`ConfigSources.PlatformSystems` and `PlatformSettings`; before the user's files, so a diagnostic points at `built-in/systems.linux.toml`; `ConfigWriter` takes a default from the OS's file first). They were first made from the Windows profiles on 2026-11-03 and are maintained by hand, like the rest of the defaults. Their rules:

- **The same profile on both:** a profile for the same emulator, doing the same thing, has the same id and name in `emulators.windows.toml` and `emulators.linux.toml`, with each OS's program, core and arguments, so `systems.toml`, the user's files and per-game overrides work on either. Keep them in step: a new emulator on both gets one id. The launcher's own (`dolphin`, `pcsx2`, `ppsspp`, `vita3k`, `eden`) keep their flags on Linux; `eden`'s places (`eden` on the PATH, `Eden*.AppImage`) aren't checked against a Linux install yet.
- **Places:** a profile lists its program's names on the PATH, then its AppImages under `{emulators}` and its Flatpak under `{flatpak}`. A RetroArch profile is `executable = "{retroarch}"` and `core = "{retroarch_cores}/<core>_libretro.so"`. MAME's profiles start in `{home}/.mame` (made at launch if it's missing: Finding programs).
- **Left out:** Windows programs run through Wine or Proton. A system whose default has no Linux build keeps its Windows profile in Linux's file ("Windows only": Future Pinball, KytyPS5, CSpect), never found, so its games still show.
- **A system's emulators on Linux** are its Linux profiles, its Windows default kept when Linux has it; a variant has its system's. **Its extensions** lose Windows' own file types (`.bat`, `.cmd`, `.lnk`, `.url`) and gain Linux's: Desktop is `.AppImage`, `.desktop` and `.sh`, Steam `.desktop` and `.sh`, and systems that run shortcuts gain `.desktop` and `.sh`.
- **On 2026-11-03:** 428 profiles, all but 5 with Windows' ids and names (the new ones are DeSmuME, Lightspark, LinApple, Nestopia UE and Plastic, Linux-only programs), and 87 systems with Linux values. `EmulatorPlacesTests` checks the Linux defaults load without a diagnostic, keep every system, offer only Linux profiles, give a variant its system's, and name a shared profile as Windows does.

### Regional variants (2026-11-02)

A system whose games came in different boxes in different regions can have a **regional variant**: a system of its own, in `Defaults/systems.toml` after the one it varies, with its own id (and so its own default ROM folder), name and description, and the same extensions, emulators and scraping ids (`screenscraper_id`, `igdb_platforms`, `launchbox_platforms`), whose themes show the other regions' box. Unlike an alias (above), which only names another folder for the same system, a variant is a system of its own, with its own games and its own template; its `group` puts its games in its system's card (below, [Grouped systems](#grouped-systems-2026-11-03)), so the grid keeps one card a console. **The system a variant varies is the European one** (the owner, 2026-11-03), the region the launcher's own boxes are drawn for. PC Engine and TurboGrafx-16, and others are to follow the same way (the owner, 2026-11-02).

- **`dreamcastnajp`, Dreamcast (NA/JP)** (2026-11-02), the first: the base theme gives `dreamcast` the PAL case its ScreenScraper art is sized for (`dreamcast_jewel_case`, A7) and the variant the CD jewel case North American and Japanese games came in (`jewel_case`), with the Dreamcast's colour and look.
- **`megacd`, Mega-CD** (2026-11-03), with two: `segacd`, Sega CD, and `megacdjp`, Mega-CD (JP). Until then `segacd` ("Sega CD / Mega-CD") was one system, with `megacd` and `megacdjp` as aliases; now each of ES-DE's three folders is its own system, as ES-DE has them, and `segacd` keeps its id (its folder, its user data and its media's folder) for the North American games that are in it. Each region's games came in a box of their own, sized by ScreenScraper's scans of the owner's library (A7, "The Mega-CD and Sega CD cases"): the European ones in a double jewel case (`megacd_case`), the North American ones in Sega's long box (`segacd_case`), the Japanese ones in a CD jewel case (`jewel_case`). The owner chose a variant for Japan rather than leaving `megacdjp` an alias of either: its square covers would be cropped in both other boxes.
- **`sfc`, Super Famicom** (2026-11-03), varying `snes`: Japanese games came in a tall box, `snes_box_vertical` (A7, already sized by ScreenScraper's Japanese scans: a 478 × 864 front, a 136 × 864 spine), where European and North American games came in the landscape `snes_box_horizontal`, which `snes` keeps. `sfc` was an alias of `snes` with `superfamicom`; `superfamicom` is now `sfc`'s alias, and `snesna` stays `snes`'s. Its card is the Super Famicom in every built-in theme (Console's from `snes.py`'s `sfc` kind, the Satellaview's console and the SuFami Turbo's), with the SNES's colour and look.

**Scraping doesn't know about variants:** a variant's games are scraped with `[scraping] regions` like every system's, so which region's art they get is the user's to set, as in ES-DE and other frontends; a user who wants a variant's art puts its games in its folder and scrapes with that region first. `ConfigLoaderTests` checks each variant's extensions, emulators, scraping ids, maker and year stay its system's, and its folder is its id's. Its description is its system's (ES-DE has no `dreamcastnajp`).

**Moving a game to a variant** (or from one) moves its file to another system: its media and its user data (favourite, play history, overrides) are keyed by the system it was in and stay there, as orphans (A4). So does a game in an ES-DE `megacd` or `megacdjp` folder that was `segacd`'s before 2026-11-03: its media is still in `media/segacd/` (moving its files to `media/megacd/` or `media/megacdjp/` brings them back) and its favourite and play history are still under `segacd`. The same goes for a game in an `sfc` or `superfamicom` folder, `snes`'s before 2026-11-03 (when `snes` had no folder of its own name, an alias folder was its only one): its media is in `media/snes/`. Not done: carrying either across.

### Grouped systems (2026-11-03)

A system with `group = "<system id>"` has no card of its own while `[display] group_systems` is on (the default): its games are in that system's card, as in Batocera's and RetroBat's grouped systems (`<group>` in their `es_systems.cfg`). The built-in groups are the regional variants' alone (`dreamcastnajp` in `dreamcast`; `segacd` and `megacdjp` in `megacd`; `sfc` in `snes`). RetroBat also groups add-ons and families (Mega-CD and 32X under Mega Drive, the FDS under NES), which would hide the Console theme's cards for them, and Batocera's users have been surprised by groups an update added. A user can group any system under another in systems.toml.

- **The card** is the group's system's: its name, colour, look, logo, card and options (X). Its subtitle counts every member's games, and its details (Y) list each system with its games. It shows while any member would (`hide_empty_systems`), and sorts as its system (`Screens/Navigator.SystemCards`). So a group whose system has no games but a variant has (an empty `dreamcast` folder, 4 games in `dreamcastnajp`) is still the Dreamcast's card, counting and showing the variant's games alone: the card stays where the console is, and doesn't turn into the variant's when the other folder empties. (If the group's system is disabled, the variant has its own card: Checks, below.)
- **Inside** is one list of every member's visible games, merged and sorted together in the group's system's `games_sort` (or settings.toml's) and shown in its `games_layout`, as All games' list is (A4 Grid queries). Each game is in its own system's template (the variant's box), with its system named in the overlay and the list. Batocera shows each child as a folder beside the parent's games; one list is what a variant wants (one console's games, each in its region's box), and needs no third screen. A grouped system's own `games_layout`, grid size and sort aren't used while it's grouped (its page says so).
- **A game stays its own system's:** it launches with that system's emulator and scrapes with its scraping ids, and its favourite, overrides and play history are keyed by it (A4). Favourites, Recently played and All games list it once, under its system. `--start-system` with a grouped system enters its group's card.
- **Checks** (each a warning, and the system keeps its own card): the group must name a known system, not the system itself, and not one that is grouped itself (groups don't nest, as in Batocera). A group whose system is disabled is only noted: the variant carries on alone.
- **Turning it off:** `group_systems = false` (Settings > Appearance > Group regional variants) gives every system its own card, and `group = ""` takes one system out of its built-in group. Two places write it: the group's own page (X on its card) has a row for each system grouped under it, "<variant> in this card", which stays after the variant is taken out so it can be put back there; and the variant's own page (Settings > ROM folders, since a grouped variant has no card) has "Show under <system>". The built-in group is kept as `SystemConfig.DefaultGroup`, so the page can put it back by removing the key.
- **Themes** need nothing new: a group uses its system's card and look, and each game its own system's template ([THEMING.md](THEMING.md)).
- **Not done:** a group with no system of its own (Batocera's placeholder groups, such as Amiga 500, 1200 and 4000 under `amiga`), and a folder view inside a group.

### Windows and Steam games

Three built-in systems hold games and programs that are their own files, with ES-DE's ids so its folders work: Windows and Steam (2026-10-01), and Desktop Apps (2026-10-03), ES-DE's folder for shortcuts to programs that aren't games, which launch and scrape as Windows' do:

```toml
[systems.windows]                    # {rom_root}/windows
name = "Windows"
manufacturer = "Microsoft"
extensions = [".bat", ".cmd", ".lnk", ".url"]   # no .exe: a game's own folder would show its uninstaller and tools
emulator = "run-file"
screenscraper_id = 138               # PC Windows (from memory, unverified like the catalogue's)
igdb_platforms = [6]                 # PC (Microsoft Windows)
steam_store = true                   # looked up on the Steam store, by title

[systems.steam]                      # {rom_root}/steam: the .url files Steam makes (Manage, Add desktop shortcut)
name = "Steam"
manufacturer = "Valve"
extensions = [".bat", ".cmd", ".lnk", ".url"]
emulator = "run-file"
screenscraper_id = 138
igdb_platforms = [6]
steam_store = true

[systems.desktop]                    # {rom_root}/desktop
name = "Desktop Apps"
manufacturer = "Microsoft"
extensions = [".bat", ".cmd", ".lnk", ".url"]
emulator = "run-file"
screenscraper_id = 138
igdb_platforms = [6]
steam_store = true
```

- Shortcuts, batch files and internet shortcuts launch as any `run_file` game (A1 Platform). A shortcut's program is followed in the game's job, so the game has ended when it and everything it started have.
- **A Steam game's shortcut** (in either system: it's the file that counts, not the system) is handed to Steam and followed through the Steam client's state until Steam says the game has ended (A1 Launching). The game's processes aren't the launcher's: Steam starts them, after updating the game or syncing its saves if it must.
- **On Linux** (L3b, 2026-11-04) Steam's shortcut is a `.desktop` file, `Exec=steam steam://rungameid/<id>`: the app id is the first `steam://` argument of its `Exec` line, whatever program it's handed to (`steam`, `flatpak run com.valvesoftware.Steam`, `xdg-open`), and the shortcut runs its own command, as any `.desktop` game does (A5 Finding programs). So a shortcut made by Steam's own package, run where only Flathub's Steam is installed, fails to start, saying `steam` wasn't found (the owner's choice: it's the shortcut's command). Steam's state comes from `registry.vdf` (A1 `Platform`, `LinuxSteamClient`).
- A non-Steam game added to Steam has a 64-bit game id in its shortcut, which Steam doesn't report on, so it isn't followed (it counts as ended at once, like any shortcut handed to a program already running). Other stores' URLs (Epic's `com.epicgames.launcher://`) aren't followed either.
- Titles come from the file names (`Half-Life 2.url` is "Half-Life 2"), and scraping searches by title. The app id isn't used for scraping: the Steam store provider (`"steam"`, opt-in) also finds the game by its title, so a shortcut to anything (a `.lnk`, a game bought elsewhere) can get Steam's official art. `provider = "steam"` with `fallback = ["screenscraper", "launchbox", "igdb", "steamgriddb"]` asks it first for Windows and Steam games; every other system then starts with ScreenScraper.

### LaunchBox Games DB

LaunchBox's Games DB (2026-11-01, provider `"launchbox"`) has no API. LaunchBox publishes its whole database once a day as `https://gamesdb.launchbox-app.com/Metadata.zip` (about 110 MB; `Metadata.xml` 520 MB, `Mame.xml`, `Files.xml`, `Platforms.xml`), and its images at `https://images.launchbox-app.com/<file name>`.

- **The local copy** (`LaunchBoxDatabase`): the dump is streamed to `CacheDir/launchbox/Metadata.zip.part`, then read with `XmlReader` (never a DOM) into an SQLite index, `launchbox.db.tmp`, which replaces `launchbox.db` with one move once it's built; the zip is then deleted. It keeps every game (190,165 on 2026-11-01) with its alternate names (and their regions), only the images of the types a media kind uses (1.1 million of 1.35 million), the file list's entries named unlike their games (25,000), and MAME's short names. About 270 MB, mostly the games' overviews (110 MB). On the owner's machine the download took about 1 s and the index 10 s.
- **When:** the first time a scrape (`PrepareAsync`) or a lookup needs it, then when the copy is a week old (`CheckInterval`): a conditional request (`If-None-Match` with the dump's ETag, and `If-Modified-Since`), which LaunchBox answers with 304 when it has nothing newer. Settings > Scraping > LaunchBox Games DB shows the copy (its games, when it was published and checked), and A on it checks at once; `odyssey-scrape launchbox-update [--force]` and `launchbox-info` do the same. One update runs at a time and every caller joins it; a caller can stop waiting, but only `CancelUpdate` (the job card's cancel) stops it. Readers keep the old copy until the move (a reader-writer lock around each query and the move). The app shows an update as a job card, whatever started it (`LibraryJobs.LaunchBoxKind`), sharing one `LaunchBoxDatabase` between its scrape services.
- **Failures:** a download is retried twice (after 10 s, then 20 s) on a network error, a server error or 60 s without a byte; a refusal isn't. A failed update keeps the copy there is; with none, LaunchBox rests for an hour (`ProviderFailure.Closed`) and the batch carries on with the other providers. A manual search before the first download starts it and says so.
- **A cache:** `launchbox.db` has no migrations. Its `meta` table holds `schema` (`SchemaVersion`), the ETag, the publish date, when it was checked and the games; a copy of another schema, or one that can't be read, counts as none and is downloaded again. Deleting the folder costs one download.
- **Lookups** (`LaunchBoxScraper`), only among the system's `launchbox_platforms`, in order: the file's name without its extension in the file list; on Arcade, a MAME set's short name in `Mame.xml` (`pacman` is "Pac-Man (Midway)"); the name either gives is looked up as a title, its trailing `(...)` and `[...]` dropped. Else the ROM's title: every name and alternate name of those platforms is kept in memory (per set of platforms, until the copy changes), normalised (`TitleMatcher.Normalise`), and scored with `TitleMatcher.SimilarityOfNormalised` (an alternate name 0.01 behind); 0.85 is a match. Ties go to a name written exactly as the title, then to the lowest id: LaunchBox's first entry, the original rather than a hack named like it ("Chrono Trigger+" normalises to Chrono Trigger). A file-list or MAME match is method `filename`, a title one `search`. The game's id is LaunchBox's `DatabaseID`.
- **Regions,** as ScreenScraper's (`ScreenScraperScraper.RegionOrder`: `[scraping] regions`, then World, USA, Europe, ScreenScraper's own, Japan). LaunchBox's region names map to ScreenScraper's codes: World `wor`, North America and United States `us`, Canada `ca`, Europe `eu`, United Kingdom `uk`, Germany `de`, France `fr`, Spain `sp`, Italy `it`, The Netherlands `nl`, Sweden `se`, Finland `fi`, Norway `no`, Greece `gr`, Russia `ru`, Japan `jp`, Asia `asi`, China `cn`, Korea `kr`, Australia `au`, Oceania `oce`, Brazil `br`. South America and Hong Kong have no code and rank after every wanted region; an image with no region ranks as World's.
  - **Each media kind** takes the first of its types the game has (cover: `Box - Front`, `Box - Front - Reconstructed`, `Fanart - Box - Front`; back: `Box - Back`, `Box - Back - Reconstructed`; spine `Box - Spine`; screenshot: `Screenshot - Gameplay`, `Screenshot - Game Title`; logo `Clear Logo`; hero `Fanart - Background`; label: `Cart - Front`, `Disc`), and of that type the image in the best-ranked region, as ScreenScraper's `PickMedia` does.
  - **The title** is the game's name, ranked as World's, unless an alternate name in a region wanted before World has one: with Japan first, Streets of Rage 2 is "Bare Knuckle II: Shitou e no Requiem" (and LaunchBox's European name for it is "Streets of Rage II").
- **The rest:** the release date is the date part of `ReleaseDate` (else `ReleaseYear`), the rating `CommunityRating` out of 5, players "1-n" from `MaxPlayers`, and the genres, developers and publishers LaunchBox separates with `;` are joined with commas, as the other providers' are. The overview is English only.
- **A scrape's response** (and `--save-responses`) is the game as `LaunchBoxDatabase.Load` writes it (JSON: its fields, alternate names and images), which `LaunchBoxScraper.Parse` reads offline, so `ScrapedRestore` restores it like the others'.

### Launch placeholders (expanded at launch time)

| Placeholder | Expands to |
|---|---|
| `{rom}` | Absolute ROM path, with native separators. |
| `{rom_dir}` | The folder containing the ROM. |
| `{rom_file}` | The ROM's file name. |
| `{rom_stem}` | The ROM's file name without its extension. |
| `{system}` | The system id. |
| `{emulator}` | The expanded `executable`. |
| `{emulator_dir}` | The folder of the expanded `executable`. |
| `{core}` | The profile's expanded `core`. Using it in a profile without one is a validation error. |
| `{title_id}` | The game's title id, read from its file at launch (2026-10-28; [Vita3K's games](#vita3ks-games)): `[PCSF00024]` in its name, else a small file's text, else a `param.sfo`. A game with none can't be launched with it. |
| `{home}`, `{rom_root}`, `{<variable>}` | Resolved already at config load. |

Write `{{` or `}}` for a literal brace.

### Expansion rules

- Each `args` entry becomes exactly one argument. There's no shell, and users never quote anything.
- **Quoting is the runner's job.** Windows passes a process one UTF-16 command line, so `WindowsCommandLine` quotes each argument by the Microsoft C runtime's rules (the ones `CommandLineToArgvW` and emulators' `main` use): an argument with a space, tab, newline or `"`, or an empty one, is wrapped in quotes; backslashes before a `"` are doubled and the quote escaped. Spaces, `&`, `%`, `^`, braces and any non-ASCII text arrive intact, because there's no cmd.exe and the command line is UTF-16 end to end (`CreateProcessW`). The Linux stub uses `ProcessStartInfo.ArgumentList`.
- Expansion is single-pass, so a ROM named `{x}.zip` stays literal.
- An unknown placeholder is a validation error, and an argument with a NUL character is a launch error.
- **`.bat` and `.cmd` executables are rejected.** cmd.exe re-parses their arguments, so a name like "Sonic & Knuckles" would break or inject commands.

### Paths in config

- Use forward slashes or TOML literal strings (`'C:\Emulators'`).
- Relative paths resolve against ConfigDir, or against the theme folder in `theme.toml`.
- Extension matching ignores case.

### Merge and validation

- Tables merge recursively; scalars and arrays are replaced whole.
- `enabled = false` switches off a built-in entry.
- A new id must be complete, and any missing required keys are listed. Required: `name`, `extensions` and `emulator` for a system; `name` and `executable` for an emulator.
- Ids use lower-case letters, digits, `_` and `-`.
- A TOML syntax error means the whole file is ignored, and its first diagnostic is shown.
- A semantic error disables only the offending entry: a system or emulator is left out, with an info diagnostic saying so, and a bad setting falls back to its default. A system whose `emulator` is unknown, disabled or has errors is disabled; an unknown `alt_emulators` entry is only a warning.
- Unknown keys are warnings, with "did you mean" for a key within two edits.
- **Installs are checked (M3, changed 2026-10-01).** For every emulator an enabled system uses as its `emulator`, a missing `executable`, or else a missing `core`, is a **warning** that names the systems affected: `emulators.retroarch-genesis-plus-gx.executable: the executable 'C:\RetroArch-Win64\retroarch.exe' doesn't exist, so Master System (mastersystem) and Mega Drive (megadrive) can't launch games (it's their emulator)...`. The systems stay enabled, so an unplugged drive doesn't empty the library, and launching reports the same problem. Alternatives (`alt_emulators`) aren't reported (the catalogue lists many the user doesn't have; the Emulators page shows each program's state, and launching names a missing one), nor are `run_file` profiles. Each path is checked once. `ConfigSources.FileExists = null` turns the check off (boot), and `CheckInstallsFor` limits it to some systems (the app passes the ones that have games, so a fresh install stays quiet).
- A `core` that no `args` entry uses as `{core}` is a warning.
- Every diagnostic has the file, line, column and dotted key: `user/systems.toml:3:1: error: systems.megadrive.emulator: unknown emulator 'blastemm' (did you mean 'blastem'?)`. Values from the defaults point at `built-in/<file>`.

### Writing config (M7)

The settings screen saves each change as it's made, through `ConfigWriter` and `TomlEditor`:
- **Only what changed, in the user's own files.** An edit that matches what the file says writes nothing. A key the file doesn't have is added only if its value differs from the built-in default. A key set back to its default is removed, unless its line has a comment (then it's updated, and the comment kept). "Use the default" removes the key.
- **The user's text stays as written.** Comments, blank lines, key order, quoting, line endings and a byte order mark are kept. A changed value keeps the comment after it on its line, but a multi-line array is written again on one line, so comments inside it go. A new key goes at the end of its table, before the blank lines and the comments above the next table; a new table goes at the end of the file; keys written as dotted keys stay dotted. An inline table can be changed but not added to or removed from. A file with a syntax error isn't touched: the settings screen says so, with the line.
- **Validated before writing.** The edited files are loaded as the app would load them, and an edit that brings an error the files didn't have (an unknown emulator, a bad placeholder, a wrong type) is refused with the loader's message; nothing is written. `ConfigInput` checks what the loader can't: the folder exists, the program is an `.exe` (not `.bat` or `.cmd`), a credential is one line.
- **Paths** are written with `/` (`//server/share` for a share) and with braces doubled, so a folder named `{x}` isn't read as a placeholder.
- **Atomic:** each file is written to `<file>.saving`, then moved over the original.

### Secrets

ScreenScraper account and developer credentials, the SteamGridDB API key and the IGDB (Twitch application) client id and secret live only in `ConfigDir/secrets.toml` or in `ODYSSEY_*` environment variables, which override the file value by value (M4, `ProviderAccounts`). A release carries ScreenScraper's developer credentials itself (below), and uses only those, so users only enter their own account:

```toml
[screenscraper]      # ODYSSEY_SCREENSCRAPER_DEV_ID, _DEV_PASSWORD, _USERNAME, _PASSWORD
dev_id = "..."       # developer credentials, issued by ScreenScraper (on its forum) for this software; built into a release
dev_password = "..."
username = "..."     # the user's account; optional (anonymous limits otherwise)
password = "..."

[steamgriddb]        # ODYSSEY_STEAMGRIDDB_API_KEY
api_key = "..."

[igdb]               # ODYSSEY_IGDB_CLIENT_ID, ODYSSEY_IGDB_CLIENT_SECRET
client_id = "..."
client_secret = "..."
```

- **Built in (2026-10-02).** The release workflow writes `src/Launcher.Core/Scraping/BuiltInAccounts.Release.g.cs` from the repository's `SCREENSCRAPER_DEV_ID` and `SCREENSCRAPER_DEV_PASSWORD` secrets (`tools/write-built-in-accounts.ps1`; a tag fails without them) before it builds. The file is gitignored, and implements a partial method of `BuiltInAccounts`, so a build from source has no built-in credentials and asks for them as before. Each value is XORed with a random key made for each build, so it isn't plain text in the DLL: that hides it from a casual search, not from someone determined (every request carries it), and ScreenScraper can revoke it.
